## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-04 M8 physical purge secure erase evidence gate)

- Physical purge now has an optional operator-attested secure erase evidence gate. The new `Maxio.Cluster.Rebalance.SecureEraseEvidence` validator accepts an evidence map or JSON file, validates `method`, `issued_at`, `operator`, exact `job_id`, `quarantine_id`, `manifest_hash`, and the exact physical-removal endpoint manifest before quarantine purge. It only validates and records evidence; it never performs device sanitize, broad filesystem deletion, topology mutation, deployment-config mutation, or scheduler automation.
- `PhysicalPurge.preflight/2` and `PhysicalPurge.apply/2` now carry a `secure_erase_evidence` summary through the preflight report, purge report, initial purge ledger, and final `physical_removal_purge_results`. When `require_secure_erase_evidence: true` is set, missing or invalid evidence fails closed with a `{:secure_erase_evidence_invalid, reason}` blocker before any quarantine deletion. When evidence is optional, missing evidence remains `not_collected`, and supplied invalid evidence is recorded as `invalid` without blocking purge.
- `mix maxio.rebalance.physical_purge_preflight` and `mix maxio.rebalance.physical_purge` now support `--secure-erase-evidence PATH` and `--require-secure-erase-evidence`. Text output includes `secure_erase_evidence_status`, and JSON output includes the sanitized evidence summary. Existing destructive confirmations (`--confirm-job-id` and `--confirm-quarantine-id`) remain mandatory for the apply command.
- Sensitive hardware/evidence fields are fail-closed or de-identified before persistence. Endpoint evidence containing raw `serial` or `wwid` is rejected; `serial_hash` and `wwid_hash` must be SHA256-shaped hashes; an opaque `evidence_id` may be supplied but is persisted only as `evidence_id_hash`. Regression coverage asserts raw `SERIAL-SECRET`, `WWID-SECRET`, and `RAW-EVIDENCE-ID` strings do not appear in purge reports, job results, or CLI JSON.
- Core regressions cover required missing evidence blocking purge without mutating job purge state, required valid evidence purging and recording sanitized samples, and optional invalid evidence recording `status=invalid` without blocking purge. CLI regressions cover preflight JSON reporting a required-evidence blocker, apply JSON accepting a required valid evidence file, and apply rejecting a required missing evidence file before deleting quarantine roots.
- Independent review used separate local Codex read-only lanes via `codex exec -m gpt-5.3-codex-spark -s read-only`, not Claude. The first review found 1 Medium and 2 Low issues: optional invalid evidence blocked purge, raw `evidence_id` could be persisted, and optional-invalid coverage was missing. These were fixed; follow-up review returned APPROVED with no High/Medium/Low findings. The reviewer then suggested two non-blocking CLI negative tests; those were added, and a second follow-up Codex review again returned APPROVED with no High/Medium/Low findings.
- Verification: focused `mix test test/maxio/cluster/rebalance_physical_removal_test.exs test/mix/tasks/maxio_rebalance_physical_purge_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 24 tests, 0 failures; adjacent rebalance/decommission workflow and CLI suite -> 41 tests, 0 failures; `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`; scoped `mix format --check-formatted`; scoped `git diff --check` passed with only the existing `STATUS.md` CRLF/LF warning; final `mix precommit` on the available default localhost:5432 Postgres -> 3122 tests, 0 failures (5 excluded).

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-04 Logging XML strictness)

- `PutBucketLogging` XML parsing now uses the shared `Maxio.S3.RequestParsers.XMLTree` helper instead of the legacy local `Saxy.Handler` state machine. The parser accepts only the `BucketLoggingStatus` root, preserves the existing normalized config-map contract, and rejects wrong roots, unknown or misnested elements, non-whitespace mixed text, nested leaf values, duplicate singleton fields, duplicate `LoggingEnabled`, duplicate target fields, malformed XML, missing required `TargetBucket` / `TargetPrefix`, empty `TargetBucket`, empty `TargetGrants`, malformed `Grant` / `Grantee`, unsupported email grantees, invalid TargetGrant permissions, and invalid `TargetObjectKeyFormat` shapes before handler persistence.
- Logging TargetGrant semantics are now aligned with AWS's logging-specific `TargetGrant` contract. `Maxio.S3.Logging` accepts only `READ`, `WRITE`, and `FULL_CONTROL` for server-access-log target grants; `READ_ACP` and `WRITE_ACP` now fail closed through the same domain validator used by XML, Console JSON, and direct callers. Canonical-user and group grantee validation remains layered through `Maxio.Policy.ACL`, so unsupported group URIs and invalid grants still reject without storing config.
- `TargetObjectKeyFormat` parsing is now strict: exactly one of `SimplePrefix` or `PartitionedPrefix` is allowed; `SimplePrefix` must be empty; `PartitionedPrefix` may omit `PartitionDateSource`, which normalizes to `EventTime`, or may specify exact-case `EventTime` / `DeliveryTime`. Lowercase or duplicate variants return `MalformedXML` and keep any prior logging config intact.
- Existing control-plane boundaries are preserved. Empty `BucketLoggingStatus` still disables/deletes logging; `TargetPrefix` may be an explicitly empty string; `PutBucketLogging` still reads the small control body through `ControlBody.read_and_verify!/4` with `Content-MD5` or modeled flexible checksum, enforces `x-amz-expected-bucket-owner`, rejects cross-region targets, requester-pays targets, Object Lock targets, and TargetGrants against bucket-owner-enforced targets, and never overwrites an existing config after invalid XML or invalid semantics.
- Parser regressions now cover empty config, namespaced XML, empty `TargetPrefix`, namespaced TargetGrants, partitioned key format, wrong root, duplicate `LoggingEnabled`, duplicate target fields, unknown/misnested/mixed text, missing required target fields, partitioned key format defaulting, duplicate/invalid key-format variants, empty TargetGrants, empty Grant, unsupported logging grant ACL permissions, unsupported email grantees, and grantee shape mismatches. E2E regressions prove malformed XML, missing fields, nested leaves, unsupported permissions, `READ_ACP`, email grantee, empty TargetGrants, duplicate key-format variants, invalid partition date source, and unsupported group URI all return `MalformedXML` without overwriting an existing logging config.
- The batch was checked against AWS S3 documentation for `PutBucketLogging`, `LoggingEnabled`, `TargetObjectKeyFormat`, `PartitionedPrefix`, `TargetGrant`, and `Grantee`: empty `BucketLoggingStatus` disables logging, `LoggingEnabled` requires `TargetBucket` and `TargetPrefix`, `TargetPrefix` can be empty, key-format variants are mutually exclusive, `PartitionDateSource` is `EventTime` / `DeliveryTime` with `EventTime` defaulting, TargetGrant permissions are `FULL_CONTROL` / `READ` / `WRITE`, and email grantee support is intentionally rejected for this project.
- Independent review used a separate local Codex lane via `codex exec -m gpt-5.3-codex-spark --sandbox read-only`, not Claude. The review returned APPROVED with no High/Medium/Low findings. It noted two non-blocking low-risk observations: grant-list parsing uses `grants ++ [grant]` for a small XML list, and pure-whitespace `TargetPrefix` remains allowed unless the project chooses to make that stricter later.
- Verification: parser `mix test test/maxio/s3/request_parsers/logging_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 10 tests, 0 failures; logging E2E `mix test test/maxio/s3/logging_e2e_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 23 tests, 0 failures; adjacent checksum/expected-owner/requester-pays/object-lock/logging suite -> 126 tests, 0 failures; `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`; scoped `mix format --check-formatted`; scoped `git diff --check`; first final precommit attempt with `PGPORT=15432` was invalid because no Postgres was listening on that port; final valid `mix precommit` on the available default localhost:5432 Postgres -> 3116 tests, 0 failures (5 excluded).

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-04 Notification XML strictness)

- `PutBucketNotificationConfiguration` XML parsing now uses the shared `Maxio.S3.RequestParsers.XMLTree` helper instead of the legacy local `Saxy.Handler` state machine. The parser accepts only the `NotificationConfiguration` root, preserves the existing normalized config-map contract, and rejects wrong roots, unknown or misnested elements, non-whitespace mixed text, nested leaf values, duplicate singleton fields, duplicate `Event` values, malformed XML, non-empty `EventBridgeConfiguration`, invalid `Filter/S3Key/FilterRule` shapes, malformed destination ARNs, and invalid event names before handler persistence.
- `Maxio.S3.Notification` now enforces the same client-facing semantics for XML, Console JSON, direct callers, and AWS destination validation inputs. Current AWS `s3:ObjectAnnotation:*`, `s3:ObjectAnnotation:Put`, and `s3:ObjectAnnotation:Delete` event enums are accepted; duplicate events are rejected; Queue/Topic/Lambda destination ARNs must match the expected AWS ARN service/account/resource shape; SQS/SNS FIFO `.fifo` destinations fail closed in the domain layer; and key filter values remain URL-encoding checked, wildcard-free, overlap-checked, and capped at the S3 object-key filter boundary.
- `Maxio.S3.NotificationDestinationValidator.AWS.validate/3` now calls `Notification.validate_client_config/1` and returns `{:error, :invalid_notification_configuration}` for invalid notification configs instead of raising `Maxio.S3.Error`. This keeps the validator behaviour fail-closed and predictable even when called directly in tests or future operator tooling.
- Existing control-plane and delivery boundaries are preserved: empty `NotificationConfiguration` still disables/deletes bucket notifications; whitespace-only `EventBridgeConfiguration` is accepted while child/text content is rejected; valid `CloudFunctionConfiguration/CloudFunction`, Queue, Topic, filters, EventBridge, destination validation, checksum/chunked body validation, expected-bucket-owner, IAM action, and invalid-request no-overwrite behavior continue to pass through the existing handler and domain contracts.
- Parser regressions now cover ObjectAnnotation events, whitespace-only EventBridge configuration, wrong root, unknown elements, mixed text, nested destination leaves, duplicate IDs/destinations/events/EventBridge, invalid events, malformed FilterRule names, duplicate/missing FilterRule fields, malformed destination ARNs, FIFO SQS/SNS ARNs, overlong filter values, wildcard/unencoded filter values, and decoded-overlap detection. E2E regressions prove malformed XML, invalid events, invalid/non-FIFO/FIFO destination ARNs, duplicate events, nested leaves, non-empty EventBridge configuration, and overlong filters return `MalformedXML` without overwriting an existing notification config. Console settings coverage now proves invalid JSON, bad event names, bad ARNs, FIFO ARNs, and duplicate events are rejected without writing config.
- The batch was checked against AWS S3 documentation for `PutBucketNotificationConfiguration`, `NotificationConfiguration`, `QueueConfiguration`, `TopicConfiguration`, `LambdaFunctionConfiguration`, `NotificationConfigurationFilter`, and `FilterRule`: empty config disables notifications, PUT replaces atomically, destinations are validated unless `x-amz-skip-destination-validation` is set, EventBridge has an empty element body, Queue/Topic/Lambda configurations require destination ARN plus at least one supported event, current event enums include ObjectAnnotation events, and key-name filters are limited to `prefix`/`suffix` with no overlapping filters.
- Independent review used a separate local Codex lane via `codex exec -m gpt-5.3-codex-spark --sandbox read-only`, not Claude. The first review found 1 Medium issue: SQS/SNS FIFO `.fifo` ARNs were still accepted by the new domain ARN validator. The finding was fixed by rejecting `.fifo` in `Notification`, adding XML/E2E/Console regressions, and making the AWS validator return `:invalid_notification_configuration` for invalid normalized configs. Follow-up independent Codex review returned APPROVED with no High/Medium/Low findings.
- Verification with `PGPORT=15432`: parser `mix test test/maxio/s3/request_parsers/notification_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 12 tests, 0 failures; notification E2E `mix test test/maxio/s3/notification_e2e_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 53 tests, 0 failures; Console settings `mix test test/maxio_web/live/bucket_live_settings_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 48 tests, 0 failures; combined parser/E2E/Console rerun after FIFO fix -> 113 tests, 0 failures; notification destination/delivery/outbox/ledger/mix-task adjacent suite -> 41 tests, 0 failures; `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`; scoped `mix format --check-formatted`; scoped `git diff --check`; final `mix precommit` -> 3114 tests, 0 failures (5 excluded).

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-04 Replication XML strictness)

- `PutBucketReplication` XML parsing now uses the shared `Maxio.S3.RequestParsers.XMLTree` helper instead of its local permissive `Saxy.Handler` state machine. The parser accepts only the `ReplicationConfiguration` root, preserves the existing normalized config-map contract, and rejects wrong roots, unknown or misnested elements, non-whitespace mixed text, nested leaf values, duplicate singleton fields, malformed XML, lowercase enums, invalid integers, and non-canonical XML element spellings before handler persistence.
- Replication rule filtering is now aligned with the documented S3 V2 shape. `Filter` can be empty and is stored as `%{}` to mean all objects; otherwise it must contain exactly one `Prefix`, `Tag`, or `And`. `And` requires at least two predicates; duplicate tag keys are rejected; `Tag` requires a non-empty `Key` and a present `Value` that may be empty. `Filter` rules still require `Priority` and `DeleteMarkerReplication`, and tag-based filters now fail closed if `DeleteMarkerReplication` is `Enabled`, matching AWS's delete-marker restriction for tag filters.
- `Maxio.S3.Replication` now enforces the cross-field semantics for XML, Console JSON, and direct callers. Rule IDs must be unique when present; priorities remain unique; storage-class and status enums are exact-case; empty filters are valid; `SourceSelectionCriteria` requires `SseKmsEncryptedObjects`, requires destination `EncryptionConfiguration/ReplicaKmsKeyID`, and `ReplicaModifications` is only allowed on latest-version rules with `Filter`; `AccessControlTranslation/Owner=Destination` now requires destination `Account`. `FSX_ONTAP` was added to the accepted destination storage-class enum while `FSX_OPENZFS` remains rejected for S3 replication.
- `GetBucketReplication` rendering now round-trips empty filters as an empty `Filter` element, so the officially documented all-object V2 rule shape is not lost when configs are fetched or echoed through Console JSON.
- Existing integrity, versioning, ownership, and authorization gates are preserved: replication PUT still reads through `ControlBody.read_and_verify!/4` with `Content-MD5` or modeled flexible checksum, enforces source bucket versioning before storage, checks `x-amz-expected-bucket-owner`, and requires `iam:PassRole` for the configured role. Invalid XML or invalid semantics are rejected before `BucketConfigs.put/3`, so existing replication config is not overwritten.
- Parser regressions now cover empty `Filter`, namespaced XML, strict root/unknown/mixed/nested/duplicate failures, lowercase `Status` and `StorageClass`, duplicate IDs, invalid `Filter` combinations, duplicate tag keys, missing tag values, tag-filter delete-marker replication, SourceSelectionCriteria/KMS combinations, ReplicaModifications without latest-version `Filter`, and AccessControlTranslation without Account. E2E regressions prove invalid replication XML and semantic failures keep the prior stored config intact. Console settings tests continue to validate JSON save, echo, delete, versioning guard, and invalid-shape no-overwrite behavior through the same domain validator.
- The batch was checked against AWS S3 documentation for `PutBucketReplication`, `GetBucketReplication`, `ReplicationRule`, `ReplicationRuleFilter`, `ReplicationRuleAndOperator`, `Destination`, `SourceSelectionCriteria`, `SseKmsEncryptedObjects`, `ReplicaModifications`, `Metrics`, `ReplicationTime`, and `ReplicationTimeValue`: 1..1000 rules, `Filter`/`Priority`/`DeleteMarkerReplication` requirements, empty-filter all-object behavior, tag-filter delete-marker restriction, RTC 15-minute threshold, checksum headers, expected bucket owner, and `iam:PassRole`.
- Independent review used a separate local Codex lane via `codex exec -m gpt-5.3-codex-spark --sandbox read-only` after remote multi-agent review hit context-window limits. The review returned APPROVED with no High/Medium/Low findings across the requested focus points: empty filter, tag filter plus delete-marker replication, SourceSelectionCriteria/KMS/ReplicaModifications, AccessControlTranslation/Account, duplicate ID/Priority, lowercase enums, strict XML shape, and invalid-request no-overwrite.
- Verification with `PGPORT=15432`: parser `mix test test/maxio/s3/request_parsers/replication_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 6 tests, 0 failures; replication E2E `mix test test/maxio/s3/replication_e2e_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 27 tests, 0 failures; Console settings `mix test test/maxio_web/live/bucket_live_settings_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 48 tests, 0 failures; adjacent replication/data-plane/CLI/scheduler/Console suite -> 153 tests, 0 failures; `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`; `mix format --check-formatted` on touched Elixir/TODO files; `git diff --check` on touched files; final `mix precommit` -> 3111 tests, 0 failures (5 excluded).

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-04 Lifecycle XML strictness)

- `PutBucketLifecycleConfiguration` now parses lifecycle XML through `Maxio.S3.RequestParsers.XMLTree` instead of the legacy permissive SAX parser. The parser accepts the documented `LifecycleConfiguration` root and the legacy `LifeCycleConfiguration` spelling, preserves the existing flat internal rule map, and rejects wrong roots, unknown or misnested elements, mixed text, nested leaf nodes, duplicate singleton fields, malformed XML, and repeated unsupported `Transition` / `NoncurrentVersionTransition` containers before storage.
- Lifecycle filter and action XML is now fail-closed at the parser boundary. `Filter` must be empty, exactly one direct predicate, or one `And`; `And` requires at least two predicates; duplicate tag keys are rejected; tag `Key` is required and non-empty while tag `Value` is required and may be empty. `Expiration` requires exactly one of `Days`, `Date`, or `ExpiredObjectDeleteMarker`; date fields must be ISO8601 midnight UTC; booleans and integers are strict; `Status` and `StorageClass` values must match the supported uppercase enum exactly.
- Current and noncurrent transition semantics are now explicit. Current transitions require exactly one of `Days` or `Date` plus an exact supported lifecycle storage class. Noncurrent transitions require `NoncurrentDays` plus an exact supported storage class, allow `NoncurrentDays=0` for Glacier-family immediate transitions, and still enforce minimum 30 days for `STANDARD_IA` / `ONEZONE_IA`. `NewerNoncurrentVersions` is restricted to 1..100 where supported.
- Handler validation was hardened for both XML and non-XML callers. Lifecycle configs must have 1..1000 rules; optional IDs must be non-empty, <=255 characters, and unique; each rule must have exact `Enabled` / `Disabled` status, at least one real action, and exactly one selector path (`Filter` or legacy `Prefix`). `Prefix` and `Filter` remain mutually exclusive; size filters require a sane range; tag filters are forbidden with `AbortIncompleteMultipartUpload` and `ExpiredObjectDeleteMarker`; `NewerNoncurrentVersions` requires a filter; lowercase/non-canonical transition storage classes from Console JSON or other direct callers are rejected instead of normalized.
- Integrity and ownership boundaries are preserved. Lifecycle PUT now reads the small control body through `ControlBody.read_and_verify!/2`, so `Content-MD5` / modeled flexible checksum failures happen before parsing or persistence, and PUT/GET/DELETE lifecycle paths enforce `x-amz-expected-bucket-owner` through the shared expected-owner guard.
- New regressions prove invalid lifecycle requests do not overwrite existing configuration. E2E coverage now checks malformed XML, invalid filter/action combinations, invalid status/storage class values, repeated action blocks, nested leaves, invalid size ranges, missing selectors, empty/long/duplicate IDs, tag-filter/action conflicts, `ExpiredObjectDeleteMarker=false` combinations, noncurrent transition minimum days, and lowercase direct-handler storage classes. Console settings coverage was aligned with the stricter selector requirement: valid JSON uses explicit `has_filter`, and missing `Filter` / legacy `Prefix` is rejected without writing config.
- The batch was checked against AWS S3 lifecycle API documentation for `PutBucketLifecycleConfiguration`, `LifecycleRule`, `LifecycleRuleFilter`, `LifecycleRuleAndOperator`, lifecycle `Tag`, `Expiration`, `Transition`, `NoncurrentVersionExpiration`, `NoncurrentVersionTransition`, `AbortIncompleteMultipartUpload`, checksum headers, and expected bucket owner behavior.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-04 ObjectLock XML strictness)

- `PutBucketObjectLockConfiguration`, `PutObjectRetention`, and `PutObjectLegalHold` XML parsing now use `Maxio.S3.RequestParsers.XMLTree` instead of the legacy hand-written SAX state machine. The parser enforces the documented roots (`ObjectLockConfiguration`, `Retention`, `LegalHold`), structural nesting, singleton fields, non-empty leaf values, mixed-text rejection, nested-leaf rejection, unknown-element rejection, and malformed XML fail-closed behavior consistently across the three Object Lock write paths.
- Bucket Object Lock config parsing now rejects duplicate `ObjectLockEnabled`/`Rule`/`DefaultRetention` children, invalid or lowercase XML retention modes, missing/empty fields, and invalid `Days`/`Years` combinations before storage. Business validation remains layered in `Maxio.S3.ObjectLock`: `ObjectLockEnabled` must normalize to `Enabled`, `DefaultRetention` requires `Mode` plus exactly one positive `Days` or `Years`, and successful configs keep the existing persisted map shape.
- Object retention XML parsing now accepts only uppercase `GOVERNANCE` / `COMPLIANCE` `Mode` values plus a non-empty `RetainUntilDate`, then delegates timestamp normalization to the existing Object Lock domain helper. Wrong roots, unknown/misnested nodes, duplicate `Mode`/`RetainUntilDate`, nested date/mode leaves, mixed text, empty values, lowercase modes, and invalid timestamps all return `MalformedXML` before object metadata mutation.
- Object legal hold XML parsing now accepts only uppercase `ON` / `OFF` `Status` values. Wrong roots, unknown/misnested nodes, duplicate `Status`, nested status leaves, mixed text, empty values, and lowercase statuses all return `MalformedXML` before object metadata mutation.
- Handler integrity and authz boundaries are preserved: bucket config still requires `Content-MD5` or a modeled flexible checksum through `ControlBody`, object retention/legal hold keep their optional checksum path, expected bucket owner and requester-pays checks still run before mutation, and the parser never touches object bytes or buffering.
- New E2E regressions prove malformed XML does not overwrite existing bucket Object Lock config and does not change an object's existing retention or legal hold metadata. Lowercase XML enum attempts now fail as `MalformedXML` and leave prior state intact, while header-side Object Lock normalization remains outside this XML parser path.
- The batch was checked against AWS API documentation for `PutObjectLockConfiguration`, `ObjectLockRule`, `DefaultRetention`, `PutObjectRetention`, and `PutObjectLegalHold`: root/body shapes, `ObjectLockEnabled`, retention `Mode`, `Days`/`Years` exclusivity, `RetainUntilDate`, legal hold `Status`, `Content-MD5` and flexible checksum headers, expected bucket owner, request payer, and governance bypass semantics.
- Independent Codex read-only review first found a medium AWS-compatibility issue: lowercase XML `Mode`/`Status` values could be normalized and persisted. The parser was tightened to reject lowercase XML enums, parser/E2E regressions were added, focused/adjacent tests and warnings-as-errors compile were rerun, and follow-up independent Codex read-only review returned APPROVED / no behavior findings. Residual noted risk: `XMLTree` intentionally normalizes by local XML names and ignores namespace URI/attributes, matching the shared strict-parser helper's current boundary.
- Verification with `PGPORT=15432`: focused `mix test test/maxio/s3/request_parsers/object_lock_test.exs test/maxio/s3/object_lock_e2e_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 22 tests, 0 failures; adjacent `mix test test/maxio/s3/request_parsers/object_lock_test.exs test/maxio/s3/object_lock_e2e_test.exs test/maxio/s3/request_payment_e2e_test.exs test/maxio/s3/expected_bucket_owner_e2e_test.exs test/maxio/s3/checksum_e2e_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 108 tests, 0 failures; `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`; final `mix precommit` -> 3097 tests, 0 failures (5 excluded).

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-04 CompleteMultipartUpload/DeleteObjects mutating XML strictness)

- `CompleteMultipartUpload` now uses `Maxio.S3.RequestParsers.XMLTree` instead of the legacy permissive SAX handler. The parser requires the `CompleteMultipartUpload` root, at least one `Part`, singleton `PartNumber` and `ETag` per part, supported optional checksum singleton fields, exact integer `PartNumber` values in `1..10000`, and rejects wrong roots, unknown/misnested elements, duplicate singleton fields, mixed text, nested leaf nodes, empty required fields, empty checksum fields, and malformed XML before the multipart commit path.
- Multipart semantic validation remains layered correctly: the parser owns request-shape and scalar checks, while `Maxio.Multipart.complete/3` continues to own part existence, ETag matching, ascending part order, and checksum consecutiveness. A malformed complete-multipart XML request now returns `MalformedXML`, does not commit an object, and leaves the multipart upload retryable.
- `DeleteObjects` now uses `XMLTree` with a strict `Delete` root, at least one `Object`, required singleton non-empty `Key`, optional singleton `VersionId`, and optional singleton `Quiet` that must be `true` when present. Wrong roots, unknown/misnested elements, duplicate singleton fields, mixed text, nested leaf nodes, missing/empty keys, invalid `Quiet`, and malformed XML fail before any per-key reducer can delete objects.
- The AWS `ObjectIdentifier` conditional delete fields `ETag`, `LastModifiedTime`, and `Size` are no longer silently ignored. They now fail closed as structured `NotImplemented` with HTTP 501, preserving existing objects until conditional-delete semantics are implemented explicitly.
- Existing integrity boundaries are preserved: DeleteObjects still goes through the control-body path that requires `Content-MD5`, and CompleteMultipartUpload keeps its existing optional body checksum/MD5 behavior. The strict parsers sit behind those body-read limits and do not introduce object-body buffering.
- The batch was checked against AWS API documentation for `CompleteMultipartUpload`, `CompletedPart`, `DeleteObjects`, and `ObjectIdentifier`: root/body shapes, part fields, checksum field names, `PartNumber` range, documented complete-multipart error families, DeleteObjects `Quiet`, `VersionId`, required keys, 1000-key request limit, `Content-MD5`, expected bucket owner, and conditional delete fields.
- Independent Codex read-only review returned APPROVED / no blocking findings. Its only residual was handler-level E2E coverage initially exercising only the `ETag` conditional-delete field; regressions were added for `LastModifiedTime` and `Size`, then the focused suite and warnings-as-errors compile were rerun.
- Verification with `PGPORT=15432`: direct parser `mix test test/maxio/s3/request_parsers/complete_multipart_upload_test.exs test/maxio/s3/request_parsers/delete_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 19 tests, 0 failures; focused `mix test test/maxio/s3/request_parsers/complete_multipart_upload_test.exs test/maxio/s3/request_parsers/delete_test.exs test/maxio/s3/multipart_integration_test.exs test/maxio/s3/delete_objects_versioning_test.exs test/maxio/s3/delete_objects_authz_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 63 tests, 0 failures; adjacent `mix test test/maxio/s3/request_parsers/complete_multipart_upload_test.exs test/maxio/s3/request_parsers/delete_test.exs test/maxio/s3/multipart_integration_test.exs test/maxio/s3/delete_objects_versioning_test.exs test/maxio/s3/delete_objects_authz_test.exs test/maxio/s3/request_payment_e2e_test.exs test/maxio/s3/object_lock_e2e_test.exs test/maxio/s3/expected_bucket_owner_e2e_test.exs test/maxio/s3/checksum_e2e_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 165 tests, 0 failures; `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`; final `mix precommit` -> 3096 tests, 0 failures (5 excluded).

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-04 PublicAccessBlock/OwnershipControls/Website XML strictness)

- `PublicAccessBlock`, `OwnershipControls`, and `Website` control-plane XML parsing now share `Maxio.S3.RequestParsers.XMLTree` as an explicit batch dependency. The helper enforces root elements, structural nesting, singleton duplicates, unknown elements, non-whitespace mixed text, nested leaf text, and malformed XML consistently for these strict parsers.
- `PutPublicAccessBlock` now parses strict `PublicAccessBlockConfiguration` XML with the four documented optional booleans: `BlockPublicAcls`, `IgnorePublicAcls`, `BlockPublicPolicy`, and `RestrictPublicBuckets`. Missing booleans still default to `false`; AWS example-compatible uppercase/lowercase boolean values are accepted; invalid, duplicate, unknown, misnested, mixed-text, and nested-leaf input fails as `MalformedXML` without overwriting existing config.
- `PutBucketOwnershipControls` now parses strict `OwnershipControls > Rule > ObjectOwnership` XML, accepts only `BucketOwnerEnforced`, `BucketOwnerPreferred`, and `ObjectWriter`, and rejects unknown, duplicate, misnested, mixed-text, nested-leaf, and multi-rule input fail-closed without mutating prior config. The handler requires either `Content-MD5` or a modeled flexible checksum, and the chunked trailer success regression now proves flexible-only trailer acceptance with `auto_content_md5: false`.
- `PutBucketWebsite` now parses strict `WebsiteConfiguration` XML while preserving the existing internal map shape. Top-level singleton sections, `IndexDocument`, `ErrorDocument`, `RedirectAllRequestsTo`, `RoutingRules`, `RoutingRule`, `Condition`, and `Redirect` all reject unknown children, misnesting, duplicate singleton fields, mixed text, and nested leaf nodes before handler validation. Existing handler validation still owns semantic combinations such as redirect-all exclusivity, required index document, routing rule redirect checks, and the 50-rule cap.
- `test/support/sigv4_request.ex` now auto-injects `Content-MD5` for these control-plane PUT helpers by default, with `auto_content_md5: false` preserved for negative and flexible-only checksum tests. This keeps ordinary tests aligned with S3 integrity requirements while making missing-integrity regressions explicit.
- The batch was checked against AWS API documentation for PutPublicAccessBlock, PutBucketOwnershipControls, OwnershipControlsRule, PutBucketWebsite, and Website data types: root/container shapes, `Content-MD5` and flexible checksum headers, expected bucket owner headers, PublicAccessBlock booleans, ObjectOwnership values, Website redirect/routing-rule structures, redirect-all exclusivity, and the 50-rule / 128 KiB Website limit.
- Independent Codex read-only review first reported no parser/handler blocking findings, but called out the untracked `XMLTree` helper as a batch hygiene risk and the OwnershipControls flexible-only trailer success coverage as not explicit enough. The trailer test was tightened with `auto_content_md5: false`, focused/adjacent tests were rerun, and a follow-up independent Codex read-only review returned APPROVED / no blocking findings.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-04 Encryption/Tagging/Versioning legacy XML strictness)

- A shared `Maxio.S3.RequestParsers.XMLTree` helper now backs this batch's control-plane XML parsing, so legacy SAX-style permissive parsers can enforce root elements, structural nesting, singleton duplicates, unknown elements, non-whitespace mixed text, nested leaf text, and malformed XML consistently.
- `PutBucketEncryption` now uses the shared control-body integrity path and requires either `Content-MD5` or a supported flexible checksum before parsing or storing configuration. Missing integrity, bad MD5, bad checksum headers, bad checksum trailers, and bad aws-chunked trailer signatures fail before any existing bucket encryption config is overwritten.
- Bucket encryption XML parsing is now strict for `ServerSideEncryptionConfiguration > Rule`. It accepts `KMSMasterKeyID` plus AWS example-compatible `KMSKeyID`, validates `BucketKeyEnabled` as explicit `true` / `false`, recognizes `BlockedEncryptionTypes` as an explicit fail-closed `NotImplemented`, and fails multiple rules as `NotImplemented` without mutating prior config.
- `PutBucketTagging` and `PutObjectTagging` now parse strict `Tagging > TagSet > Tag > Key/Value` XML. Empty `TagSet` remains valid for object-tag delete semantics, empty `Value` is preserved, and empty `Key` still reaches tag validation as `InvalidTag` instead of being rewritten to `MalformedXML`.
- `PutBucketVersioning` now parses strict `VersioningConfiguration` XML with optional singleton `Status` (`Enabled` / `Suspended`) and `MfaDelete` (`Enabled` / `Disabled`). Empty config remains a no-op, and `MfaDelete` without `Status` still reaches the handler's structured malformed-XML guard.
- The batch was checked against AWS API documentation for PutBucketEncryption, PutBucketTagging, PutObjectTagging, and PutBucketVersioning: root/container shapes, Content-MD5/flexible checksum headers, expected bucket owner behavior, object tag limits/empty tag-set semantics, and MFADelete/Status constraints.
- Independent Codex read-only review reported no blocking findings. Its only gap was missing explicit E2E coverage for multiple bucket-encryption `<Rule>` input preserving existing config; that regression was added and rerun.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-04 CORS control-plane strict XML parity)

- `PutBucketCors` now uses the shared control-plane body reader/checksum path and requires `Content-MD5`, while `Put/Get/DeleteBucketCors` also enforce `x-amz-expected-bucket-owner`. Missing or bad integrity headers and wrong expected owners fail before CORS config persistence, preserving the existing bucket config.
- The CORS XML parser is now fail-closed instead of permissive. It requires a `CORSConfiguration` root with at least one `CORSRule`, rejects unknown root/rule children, rejects misnested elements and mixed non-whitespace text, rejects duplicate singleton `ID` / `MaxAgeSeconds`, rejects non-integer `MaxAgeSeconds`, and rejects empty required `AllowedMethod` / `AllowedOrigin` / `AllowedHeader` / `ExposeHeader` values before handler validation.
- Handler validation now rejects an empty rule set, keeps the existing 100-rule cap, requires non-empty methods/origins, validates method names, enforces at-most-one `*` in origin/header patterns, rejects negative `MaxAgeSeconds`, and caps optional rule IDs at 255 characters.
- Console direct multipart CORS helper keeps the correct boundary after empty config hardening: `:none` / `[]` may be used as an input to merge a generated browser-upload rule, but the final rule set still goes through the real `Maxio.S3.Handlers.Cors.validate!/1` before storage.
- Browser preflight behavior remains locked: direct multipart managed rules allow browser preflight for POST/PUT/GET/DELETE, expose `ETag`, and now explicitly assert `Access-Control-Allow-Headers` echoes the requested allowed headers. Actual object responses continue to omit `Access-Control-Allow-Methods`.
- The batch was checked against AWS CORS API/docs and MinIO CORS documentation: root `CORSConfiguration`, 1..100 rules, first matching rule, allowed methods/origins/headers wildcard constraints, requested-header preflight matching, and per-bucket CORS behavior.
- Independent Codex read-only review reported no blocking findings. Its only non-blocking gap was missing direct multipart preflight `Access-Control-Allow-Headers` assertion; that regression was added and rerun.
- Verification with `PGPORT=15432`: focused `mix test test/maxio/s3/request_parsers/cors_test.exs test/maxio/s3/cors_e2e_test.exs test/maxio/console/direct_multipart_upload/cors_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 24 tests, 0 failures; adjacent `mix test test/maxio/s3/request_parsers/cors_test.exs test/maxio/s3/cors_e2e_test.exs test/maxio/console/direct_multipart_upload/cors_test.exs test/maxio_web/live/object_live_index_test.exs test/maxio_web/live/bucket_live_settings_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 96 tests, 0 failures; `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`; `git diff --check` on touched files; final `mix precommit` -> 3063 tests, 0 failures (5 excluded).

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 MinIO DeleteReplication version hard-delete replication)

- Bucket replication config now accepts the MinIO-compatible optional `DeleteReplication` status block in PUT/GET replication XML. Missing `DeleteReplication` remains absent by default, so AWS-shaped configs are not mutated, while enabled blocks round-trip through parser, normalization, storage, and XML rendering.
- Replication event matching now keeps delete semantics split: `s3:ObjectRemoved:Delete` only matches rules with `DeleteReplication` enabled, while delete marker creation still only matches `DeleteMarkerReplication`. Negative E2E coverage locks both directions so the two controls cannot accidentally mirror each other.
- Single-object `DELETE ?versionId=` and DeleteObjects entries with `<VersionId>` now enqueue hard-delete replication tasks in the PG write-authority path. The task snapshot carries the deleted source version's bucket/key/version, size, ETag, and tags so rule matching and delivery do not depend on reading a source object that has already been deleted.
- Hard-delete delivery deletes only destination versions whose `replication_status` is `REPLICA`; a missing destination version is idempotent success, and a same-version destination object without `REPLICA` fails closed as `destination_version_conflict`. Hard-delete delivery marks only the replication outbox row and does not attempt to update deleted source-version replication status.
- Destination physical deletion is now authoritative for hard-delete delivery: `Storage.delete_version/4` must succeed before destination PG metadata is deleted and the outbox can complete. Injected storage delete-version failures leave the task failed/retryable and preserve the destination replica metadata and bytes.
- Sidecar write authority remains a documented v1 boundary for replication enqueue, but it is no longer silent: sidecar version hard delete and sidecar delete marker creation now run the replication rule matcher and emit structured warnings when a rule would match. Logger metadata now preserves and formats `write_authority` and `matched_tasks`.
- Independent Codex read-only review first confirmed the sidecar warning/test fixes but found a high-risk delivery ordering bug: PG metadata could be deleted before best-effort storage deletion. The implementation was changed to storage-first `delete_version`, a failure-injection regression was added, and second independent Codex review reported no high/medium/low findings.
- Verification with `PGPORT=15432`: focused `mix test test/maxio/s3/replication_e2e_test.exs test/maxio/s3/log_test.exs test/maxio/s3/delete_objects_versioning_test.exs test/maxio/s3/versioning_object_test.exs test/mix/tasks/maxio_replication_outbox_test.exs test/mix/tasks/maxio_replication_drain_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 47 tests, 0 failures; `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`; final `mix precommit` -> 3058 tests, 0 failures (5 excluded).

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent CSV/JSON type-error parity)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` CSV expression runtime type failures now return structured S3 XML errors before any event-stream 200 response. Nonnumeric arithmetic operands, division/modulo by zero, nonnumeric ordered comparisons, and nonnumeric numeric aggregate inputs now map to `InvalidKeyPath` with HTTP 400 instead of leaking `NotImplemented`/501.
- Mixed aggregate and non-aggregate projection lists now fail consistently across CSV and JSON executors as `UnsupportedSqlStructure` with HTTP 400. This adds the S3 Select special error code to `Maxio.S3.Error` and keeps the validation in the executor aggregate-plan preflight, before object output streaming starts.
- The batch intentionally did not implement AWS-unsupported numeric scalar functions; expressions such as unsupported function calls still fail closed. CSV `IS MISSING` also remains fail-closed as `NotImplemented`, because the currently verified AWS `MISSING` semantics are tied to JSON wildcard missing rows rather than CSV records.
- BZIP2 Select input support was re-verified as an AWS-supported feature but not landed in this batch: the evaluated Windows-local dependency path failed native compilation, and no dependency or partial BZIP2 implementation remains in the tree.
- Regression coverage now locks CSV division-by-zero, CSV nonnumeric arithmetic, CSV nonnumeric ordered comparison, CSV nonnumeric aggregate input, CSV mixed aggregate projection, and JSON Lines mixed aggregate projection to the expected XML error responses.
- Independent Codex read-only review found no high-risk issues. It reported one medium coverage gap for direct CSV nonnumeric arithmetic; that E2E regression was added and rerun before final verification.
- Verification with `PGPORT=15432`: `mix format lib/maxio/s3/error.ex lib/maxio/s3/select_csv.ex lib/maxio/s3/select_json.ex test/maxio/s3/select_object_content_e2e_test.exs`; focused `mix test test/maxio/s3/select_object_content_e2e_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 179 tests, 0 failures; adjacent `mix test test/maxio/s3/request_parsers/select_object_content_test.exs test/maxio/s3/event_stream_test.exs test/maxio/s3/select_sql_test.exs test/maxio/s3/select_object_content_e2e_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 208 tests, 0 failures; `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`; final `mix precommit` -> 3049 tests, 0 failures (5 excluded).

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent CSV UTF-8 input parity)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` CSV input now accepts valid UTF-8 data records instead of the prior ASCII-only guard. Validation happens after source chunks are reassembled into complete logical CSV records, so multibyte code points split across storage/stream chunks are not falsely rejected.
- Invalid CSV text encoding now fails as structured S3 XML `InvalidTextEncoding` with HTTP 400, matching the AWS SelectObjectContent error family where UTF-8 is the only supported text encoding.
- ScanRange safety remains fail-closed and byte-offset based: records outside the selected range are still boundary-scanned/skipped without CSV parsing or UTF-8 validation, so invalid bytes in skipped records do not leak into selected-range execution.
- Regression coverage now exercises normal UTF-8 CSV output, UTF-8 string-literal filtering, UTF-8 values with `FileHeaderInfo=USE` header-name references, GZIP-decompressed UTF-8 CSV, invalid UTF-8 -> `InvalidTextEncoding`, ScanRange skipping invalid UTF-8 outside the selected range, and a direct stream-chunk split of a multibyte UTF-8 character.
- Independent Codex read-only review found no high/medium risks. Its only low-risk gap was missing explicit multibyte-across-chunk coverage; that regression was added before the final milestone verification.
- Verification with `PGPORT=15432`: `mix format lib/maxio/s3/select_csv.ex lib/maxio/s3/error.ex test/maxio/s3/select_object_content_e2e_test.exs --check-formatted`; `git diff --check` on the touched code/test files; `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`; focused `mix test test/maxio/s3/select_object_content_e2e_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 176 tests, 0 failures; final `mix precommit` -> 3046 tests, 0 failures (5 excluded).

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 RestoreObject SELECT OutputLocation AccessControlList grant-list)

- `RestoreObject` SELECT `OutputLocation/S3/AccessControlList` now supports explicit grant-list ACLs instead of failing them closed. The parser accepts strict `AccessControlList > Grant*` XML, normalizes grants through `Maxio.Policy.ACL`, rejects empty grant lists, rejects `CannedACL` mixed with `AccessControlList`, and maps invalid permissions, unknown group URIs, missing/invalid grantee type, email grantees, and more than 100 grants to structured `MalformedXML`.
- Restore SELECT output grant-lists now reuse the existing object ACL architecture: owner grants are dropped for persisted metadata, remaining grants are classified into `private`/public canned/custom shape, stored via `ObjectWriter.put_generated!/4` in the same generated object commit, and rendered by existing `GetObjectAcl`/ACL authz paths.
- Output ACL authorization projects XML grants into an isolated target `PUT` conn as equivalent `x-amz-grant-*` headers, so the existing `s3:PutObjectAcl` secondary authorization and IAM condition keys apply to Restore-generated output. Regression coverage includes missing `PutObjectAcl` denial and a positive grant-list condition match.
- Object Ownership and PublicAccessBlock are enforced before SELECT staging/output writes: BucketOwnerEnforced rejects public/custom grant-lists while accepting owner-only `FULL_CONTROL` XML equivalent, and `BlockPublicAcls` rejects public grants before `x-amz-restore-output-path` can be emitted.
- Positive E2E now verifies an `AllUsers` READ XML grant produces a `public-read` generated object, persists explicit grant metadata, renders through `GetObjectAcl`, and allows anonymous `GET` of the generated SELECT output. Negative E2E verifies malformed grant-lists, BOE rejection, PAB rejection, and authorization rejection leave source restore metadata untouched and do not emit an output path.
- Independent Codex read-only review first found a high parser bug where `ACL.normalize_grants/1` `{:error, reason}` tuples could leak into the parsed request and later cause a 500, plus medium BOE/PAB coverage gaps. The parser now maps `{:error, _}` to malformed XML, coverage was added, and second independent Codex review reported no high/medium findings.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 RestoreObject SELECT OutputLocation SSE and attributes)

- `RestoreObject` SELECT `OutputLocation/S3` now supports generated-output attributes instead of failing them closed wholesale: explicit `CannedACL`, `Tagging`, `UserMetadata`, and `Encryption` (`AES256`, `aws:kms`, `aws:kms:dsse`) are parsed, validated, authorized, and committed onto the output object. Target bucket default SSE is also honored when `Encryption` is omitted.
- `Maxio.S3.ObjectWriter` can now write server-generated objects under SSE-S3, SSE-KMS, and DSSE-KMS. It streams the generated plaintext through the existing crypto wrappers, records plaintext visible size in PG and sidecar metadata, preserves SSE envelopes, and still rolls back object bytes if metadata commit fails.
- Restore SELECT output authorization now builds an isolated target `PUT` conn from the parsed `OutputLocation` XML. The original RestoreObject request headers no longer leak into target PutObject/PutObjectTagging authorization conditions; XML-derived tags populate `s3:RequestObjectTag/*`, XML-derived encryption populates `s3:x-amz-server-side-encryption`, and explicit CannedACL reaches the existing PutObjectAcl secondary authorization path on ACL-enabled buckets.
- Fail-closed boundaries remain explicit: `AccessControlList` grant-list output ACLs still return structured `NotImplemented`; `aws:fsx` encryption still returns structured `NotImplemented`; invalid KMS context, invalid tags, oversized metadata, BOE ACL conflicts, and PublicAccessBlock public ACL conflicts fail before staging or writing output bytes. Source object restore metadata is still not mutated by SELECT restore.
- The Restore parser now preserves user data whitespace for OutputLocation tag key/value and user metadata value while keeping structural fields such as bucket, prefix, storage class, ACL, and encryption type normalized through the existing trimmed text path.
- Independent Codex read-only review first found a high authorization projection bug and a medium parser whitespace bug; both were fixed with E2E/parser regressions. Follow-up independent Codex review reported no high/medium/low findings.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 RestoreObject SELECT OutputLocation v1)

- `RestoreObject` now implements the `Type=SELECT` request shape with `SelectParameters` and `OutputLocation/S3`. It reuses the shared `Maxio.S3.SelectRunner` execution path, stages SELECT results in a temp file before any response is sent, and writes one generated result object at `<Prefix>/<ULID>/part-00000` with `x-amz-restore-output-path`.
- SELECT restore preserves the source object's restore metadata: it does not mark the archived source object as restored. Successful SELECT output responses now use HTTP 200 with `x-amz-restore-output-path`; regular archive restores keep the existing 202/200 restore-state behavior.
- Output writes go through `Maxio.S3.ObjectWriter`, which commits generated bytes plus metadata under the configured PG/sidecar write authority and cleans up object bytes if metadata commit fails. Sidecar write/read/list authority is covered by E2E.
- Request validation is fail-closed: source and output buckets enforce Requester Pays, source `x-amz-expected-bucket-owner` is checked, output PutObject authorization is evaluated, unsupported `OutputLocation` ACL/Tagging/UserMetadata/Encryption elements return structured `NotImplemented`, and explicit `CannedACL` including `private` is rejected until output ACL semantics are implemented.
- Target bucket default SSE is intentionally fail-closed for SELECT restore output. `ObjectWriter.put_generated!/4` now requires an explicit `:sse_target`; non-nil targets raise `NotImplemented` before bytes are written, so generated objects cannot silently bypass default bucket encryption.
- `RestoreRequest` parsing now accepts current shared S3 storage-class values for SELECT `OutputLocation/S3/StorageClass` via `Maxio.S3.StorageClass.valid_values/0`, instead of a stale Restore-specific allow-list.
- Independent Codex read-only review found no high/medium findings. Its initial low follow-ups were the implicit plaintext ObjectWriter contract and narrow OutputLocation storage-class list; both were addressed, and follow-up Codex review confirmed no high/medium findings remain.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent JSON SELECT star CSV scalar parity)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` JSON execution now allows `SELECT *` with CSV output when the selected JSON row/root is scalar. JSON Lines scalar rows (`number`, `string`, `boolean`, `null`) and default JSON DOCUMENT scalar roots serialize through the shared CSV output path, so delimiter quoting, boolean/numeric formatting, null-as-empty-field, and result-size checks stay consistent with explicit JSON->CSV projections.
- Nested JSON values still fail closed before any event-stream 200 response. `SELECT *` rows that are objects/arrays and explicit projections that resolve to objects/arrays are rejected by `Maxio.S3.SelectOutput.csv_field!/1` as structured `NotImplemented` with the existing `Nested SelectObjectContent values require JSON output` message.

- The stale JSON output-projection preflight no-op was removed from `Maxio.S3.SelectJSON`, leaving nested JSON->CSV enforcement in the shared CSV serializer rather than a format-specific preflight guard.
- Test config now supports local PostgreSQL port overrides via `PGPORT`, defaults on nil/empty values, and rejects non-integer or out-of-range TCP port values with a clear configuration error. This preserves the normal 5432 default while allowing the Windows local test DB to run on `127.0.0.1:15432`.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent CSV header ScanRange parity)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` CSV execution now supports `FileHeaderInfo=USE` header-name references together with `ScanRange`. The CSV state machine parses the first non-comment header record when a query references header names, validates missing/ambiguous names fail-closed, then applies `ScanRange` only to subsequent data records by record-start byte offset.
- The previous `NotImplemented` preflight guard for `FileHeaderInfo=USE` + header-name references + `ScanRange` was removed; positional `USE`/`IGNORE` paths still skip unneeded oversized headers without parsing, while name-reference queries must parse the required header and therefore still reject oversized/invalid headers before any event-stream 200 response.
- Regression coverage now exercises header-name projection plus `WHERE` filtering under `ScanRange`, missing header names, ambiguous case-insensitive header names, and oversized required headers. Existing BZIP2 Select input remains fail-closed; a Windows-compatible streaming BZIP2 dependency was evaluated but not landed because the available package did not provide the documented Windows precompiled NIF target.
- Independent Codex read-only review reported no high/medium findings and no important low-risk issues for the state-machine and test changes.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent JSON DOCUMENT input)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` JSON input now supports `InputSerialization/JSON/Type=DOCUMENT`; an omitted JSON `Type` also follows the AWS default of `DOCUMENT`. The executor keeps JSON Lines behavior unchanged and adds a separate DOCUMENT path that accumulates one bounded input record, capped by the existing 1 MiB Select record limit, then parses and applies the same projection, predicate, aggregate, and output serialization pipeline at EOF.
- JSON DOCUMENT supports root-document selections, aliases, nested `FROM S3Object[*].path` expansion with missing-row preservation, explicit top-level array expansion via `S3Object[*][*]`, JSON output, CSV scalar/aggregate output, and GZIP-compressed JSON DOCUMENT input.

- JSON wildcard aggregate handling now skips `MISSING` rows before updating any aggregate, including `count(*)`, matching S3 Select aggregate semantics for JSON missing values. Regression coverage covers both JSON DOCUMENT and JSON Lines `FROM` paths that yield missing rows.
- Error/bounds coverage now includes malformed JSON DOCUMENT -> `JSONParsingError` and oversized JSON DOCUMENT -> `OverMaxRecordSize`, both returned as XML errors before event-stream responses.

Those tests were added, and follow-up Codex review confirmed no high/medium findings and that all low gaps were covered.

- `GetObjectAttributes` now reuses the same SSE read-header validation path as `GET Object` and `HEAD Object`. Non-delete-marker object attribute reads call `validate_sse_read_headers!/4` before conditional request evaluation, so `If-None-Match`/304 cannot bypass SSE-C customer-key validation.

Regression coverage locks missing-all headers, partial SSE-C header sets, wrong customer keys, conditional missing-key requests, successful reads with the correct key, and conditional 304 only after successful SSE-C validation.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent CSV/JSON cross-output serialization)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` now supports the AWS request shape where input and output serialization formats may differ across the implemented CSV and JSON Lines executors. The handler now routes `CSV->CSV`, `CSV->JSON`, `JSON->JSON`, and `JSON->CSV` while continuing to reject Parquet and unsupported JSON `DOCUMENT` paths before event-stream 200.
- A new shared `Maxio.S3.SelectOutput` module owns output serialization for CSV and JSON records. Both executors now reuse the same CSV quoting, `QuoteFields`, delimiter, result record-size, JSON record delimiter, and timestamp-normalization paths, avoiding duplicate CSV formatting logic in the individual executors.
- CSV input can now emit JSON Lines output. `SELECT *` emits positional keys (`_1`, `_2`, ...); explicit projections preserve aliases/header names and keep typed values from casts/date/string/conditional expressions; duplicate JSON output names, including aggregate aliases and natural duplicate projection names, fail closed as structured `InvalidKeyPath` before any event-stream response.
- JSON Lines input can now emit CSV output for explicit scalar projections and aggregate projections. CSV output remains positional, so duplicate aliases or duplicate natural projection names are allowed for CSV output; configured CSV delimiters and quoting are honored. `JSON SELECT *` to CSV remains fail-closed as `NotImplemented`, and nested JSON values still require JSON output.
- Regression coverage now exercises CSV->JSON typed projections, `SELECT *`, header/alias output names, duplicate alias/natural/aggregate-name rejection, JSON->CSV scalar projections with quoting, aggregate output with custom delimiters, duplicate aliases/natural names as positional CSV columns, and JSON `SELECT *` -> CSV fail-closed behavior.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent conversion errors and CAST TIMESTAMP)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` SQL parsing now accepts `CAST(... AS TIMESTAMP)` as a first-class value expression, alongside the already implemented `BOOL` / `BOOLEAN`, `INT` / `INTEGER`, `FLOAT` / `DOUBLE` / `DECIMAL` / `NUMERIC`, and `STRING` cast targets.
- CSV and JSON Lines Select execution now evaluate `CAST AS TIMESTAMP` through the shared `Maxio.S3.SelectTimestamp` parser. The result preserves timestamp precision and offset for projection output, can be nested into `TO_STRING`, and participates in existing timestamp-aware `WHERE`, `BETWEEN`, `IN`, equality, and ordering comparisons.
- The S3 error table now includes official SelectObjectContent conversion errors `CastFailed` and `ValueParseFailure`, both HTTP 400. Numeric/bool `CAST` conversion failures now return `CastFailed`; timestamp string parse failures now return `ValueParseFailure`. Non-scalar/path failures remain `InvalidKeyPath`, and unsupported/mixed aggregate or arithmetic unsupported cases keep their existing errors.
- CSV Select conversion tests now cover `CAST AS TIMESTAMP` projection, timestamp `WHERE` comparison, nested `TO_STRING`, `INTEGER` / `DECIMAL` / `DOUBLE` aliases, invalid timestamp parse -> `ValueParseFailure`, and nonnumeric `CAST AS INT` -> `CastFailed`.
- JSON Lines Select conversion tests mirror the CSV coverage with aliases, JSON output serialization, timestamp predicates, numeric aliases including `NUMERIC`, invalid timestamp parse -> `ValueParseFailure`, and nonnumeric cast -> `CastFailed`.
- This supersedes earlier status notes that described invalid timestamp string parsing as `InvalidKeyPath`; the current external S3 error code for timestamp parse failure is `ValueParseFailure`.
- Independent Codex review first flagged the invalid timestamp cast mapping as a medium issue. The fix added `ValueParseFailure` and then broadened the same conversion slice to `CastFailed`; final independent Codex review reported no high/medium findings, with only low-risk parity/edge-case suggestions.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent TO_STRING and UTCNOW)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` SQL parsing now supports the remaining implemented date-function slice for CSV and JSON Lines: `TO_STRING(timestamp, pattern)` and zero-argument `UTCNOW()`. Both are structured date-function value expressions and can be nested with existing date functions, projections, predicates, and dynamic expression operands.
- `Maxio.S3.SelectTimestamp` now includes a shared AWS-style timestamp formatter for `TO_STRING`. The implemented token set covers year/month/day, month text, AM/PM, 12-hour and 24-hour clocks, minute/second, fractional seconds, nanosecond-of-second, quoted literals with doubled quotes, and `X`/`x` timezone-offset forms.
- Fractional-second formatting follows the AWS S3 Select examples rather than Java left-padding semantics: `S` tokens emit the scaled fractional integer without left-padding, so `.060000000` renders as `0`, `6`, `60`, and `60000000` for `S`, `SS`, `SSS`, and `SSSSSSSSS`.
- CSV Select execution now captures one request-stable `UTCNOW` value per query execution and evaluates `TO_STRING` through the existing staged fail-closed value-expression path. Null/missing operands keep SQL null behavior, invalid timestamp operands or unsupported format pattern letters map to structured `InvalidKeyPath` before event-stream 200.
- JSON Lines Select execution supports the same `TO_STRING`/`UTCNOW` semantics and request-stable clock value. Projected formatted values are emitted as JSON strings, while invalid/non-scalar operands remain fail-closed through the shared JSON expression error mapping.
- Regression coverage now exercises parser AST/ref collection for `TO_STRING(UTCNOW(), ref)`, CSV literal formatting including quoted `T`, month text, 12-hour clocks, offset token widths, AWS fractional `S` examples, nanosecond `n`, negative offset `x` forms, stable `UTCNOW` across repeated references, invalid pattern rejection, and JSON Lines formatting over both row values and timestamp literals.
- Independent Codex read-only review first found a medium issue in `S` fractional-token padding. The formatter was corrected to AWS example semantics, focused tests were updated, and final independent Codex re-review reported no high/medium findings.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent date functions)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` SQL parsing now supports timestamp literals wrapped in backticks plus the implemented AWS date-function subset: `TO_TIMESTAMP(expr)`, `EXTRACT(part FROM expr)`, `DATE_ADD(part, quantity, timestamp)`, and `DATE_DIFF(part, left, right)`. These functions are structured as value expressions and can appear in projections and `WHERE` predicates.
- A new shared `Maxio.S3.SelectTimestamp` core preserves timestamp precision, offset, and nanosecond data through parse, render, comparison, `DATE_ADD`, and `DATE_DIFF`. Timestamp comparison uses UTC nanoseconds; `DATE_DIFF(year/month)` normalizes offsets before calendar-part comparison; month/year adds clamp invalid end-of-month targets such as leap day or month-end rollover.
- CSV Select execution now evaluates date functions through the existing staged fail-closed expression path. Timestamp projection output preserves source/result precision, invalid timestamp input maps to structured `InvalidKeyPath` before event-stream 200, null/missing operands keep SQL unknown/null behavior, and integer date quantities are enforced.
- JSON Lines Select execution supports the same date-function set and timestamp comparison semantics, emits projected timestamps as strings for JSON output, preserves timezone extraction including `TIMEZONE_HOUR` / `TIMEZONE_MINUTE`, and keeps non-scalar or invalid timestamp operands fail-closed as `InvalidKeyPath`.
- Regression coverage now exercises parser AST/ref collection for nested date functions, CSV projection and predicate date functions, nanosecond ordering and equality across fractional digit widths, leap-day/month-end `DATE_ADD`, offset-normalized `DATE_DIFF`, fractional `DATE_ADD(second)` precision preservation, CSV/JSON invalid timestamp consistency, and JSON timezone-minute extraction.
- Independent Codex read-only review first found medium issues in nanosecond truncation and offset-insensitive `DATE_DIFF(year/month)`, then a medium CSV/JSON invalid timestamp mapping inconsistency. The timestamp core and CSV error mapping were fixed; final independent Codex re-review reported no high/medium findings.
- Residual intentional limits: `TO_STRING` and `UTCNOW` remain future S3 Select date-function slices.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent predicate value expressions and CAST operands)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` SQL parsing now treats `IN` / `NOT IN` list members, `BETWEEN` / `NOT BETWEEN` bounds, and `LIKE ... ESCAPE` pattern/escape operands as full value expressions instead of raw literals. Dynamic operands can now use refs, casts, string/conditional/arithmetic expressions, and booleans while preserving the existing predicate AST shape.
- `CAST` now accepts an arbitrary value expression operand rather than only a direct field reference, and the implemented CAST type set now includes `BOOL` / `BOOLEAN` and `STRING` alongside the existing numeric aliases. SQL boolean literals `TRUE` and `FALSE` are represented as structured literal expressions.
- Referenced-field collection now descends through dynamic predicate operands and `CAST` expression operands, so CSV header validation, ScanRange/header fail-closed behavior, and JSON path/qualifier validation continue to see every field used by runtime predicates.
- CSV Select execution now evaluates dynamic `IN`, `BETWEEN`, and `LIKE` operands through the same staged expression path as projections and ordinary comparisons. Null and missing dynamic operands keep SQL three-valued predicate semantics, dynamic `LIKE ESCAPE` is validated at execution time before event-stream 200, boolean outputs serialize as `true` / `false`, and numeric `CAST(... AS STRING)` reuses the existing stable numeric formatting.
- JSON Lines Select execution now supports the same dynamic predicate operands, boolean literals, and `CAST(expression AS BOOL/STRING/INT/FLOAT)` path. JSON null/missing operands remain non-matching/unknown, non-scalar predicate operands still fail closed as `InvalidKeyPath`, and dynamic `LIKE` patterns are compiled per evaluated row using the shared SQL LIKE compiler.
- Regression coverage now exercises parser ASTs for dynamic predicate operands, boolean literals, CAST expression operands, referenced-field collection through dynamic RHS expressions, CSV dynamic predicates plus bool/string casts, and JSON dynamic predicates plus bool/string casts.
- Independent Codex read-only review reported no high/medium findings for the dynamic predicate/CAST operand batch.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent arithmetic scalar expressions)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` SQL parsing now supports arithmetic scalar expressions for the implemented CSV and JSON Lines paths: unary `+` / `-`, multiplicative `*` / `/` / `%`, additive `+` / `-`, and parenthesized value expressions with the expected precedence.
- Ordinary comparison predicates now parse the right-hand side as a value expression, so queries such as `WHERE a + b = c - 5` work instead of only comparing against raw literals. The Select AST keeps arithmetic nodes structured and preserves existing `LIKE`, `IN`, and `BETWEEN` literal contracts.
- Referenced-field collection now descends through arithmetic and unary-minus nodes, including comparison RHS expressions. CSV header validation, ScanRange/header fail-closed behavior, and JSON path/qualifier validation therefore still see every field used by arithmetic projections and predicates.
- CSV Select execution now evaluates arithmetic projections, aggregate arguments, and `WHERE` expressions through the staged expression path. Arithmetic expressions use SQL-expression null semantics internally, so empty CSV fields produce null/empty output instead of nonnumeric crashes, while nonnumeric operands and division/modulo by zero fail closed before event-stream 200.
- JSON Lines Select execution now evaluates the same arithmetic expressions in projections and predicates, returns JSON numbers for numeric results, returns JSON `null` for null arithmetic operands, skips missing arithmetic projection values, and maps nonnumeric/non-scalar/divide-by-zero failures to structured `InvalidKeyPath` before event-stream 200.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent conditional expressions)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` SQL parsing now supports conditional value expressions for the implemented CSV and JSON Lines paths: searched `CASE WHEN ... THEN ... [ELSE ...] END`, simple `CASE expr WHEN ... THEN ... [ELSE ...] END`, `COALESCE(...)`, `NULLIF(left, right)`, and the `NULL` literal.
- Conditional expressions are structured in the Select AST and can appear in ordinary projections, aggregate arguments, nested value expressions, and the left side of `WHERE` predicates. Referenced-field collection now descends through CASE conditions/results, simple CASE operands/branches, COALESCE args, and NULLIF operands so CSV header validation and JSON path validation remain fail-closed.
- CSV Select execution now evaluates conditional expressions through the same staged expression path as CAST/string functions. Conditional expressions force SQL-expression null semantics internally, so empty CSV fields are treated as SQL `NULL` inside projection-time `CASE`, `COALESCE`, and `NULLIF`, matching the existing CAST behavior while preserving plain CSV projection output semantics outside expression functions.
- JSON Lines Select execution now evaluates conditional expressions in projections and predicates, supports aliases for conditional projection output names, returns JSON `null` for unmatched CASE/no ELSE and NULLIF matches, skips missing COALESCE inputs, and rejects duplicate conditional output names before event-stream 200.
- Conditional comparison semantics stay fail-closed: `NULLIF` over JSON objects/arrays raises structured `InvalidKeyPath`, missing/null operands keep three-valued predicate behavior, and unsupported functions such as `ABS` still return structured `NotImplemented` before event-stream 200.
- Regression coverage now exercises parser ASTs for CASE/COALESCE/NULLIF/NULL, referenced-field collection through nested conditional branches, CSV searched CASE/simple CASE/COALESCE/NULLIF projection and WHERE behavior, CSV CASE without ELSE null output, JSON conditional projections/WHERE aliases, JSON CASE without ELSE null output, JSON duplicate conditional output-name rejection, and JSON NULLIF non-scalar fail-closed behavior.
- Independent Codex review initially found a medium issue around CSV projection-time conditional expressions treating empty fields as empty strings instead of SQL nulls. The evaluator now forces conditional-expression mode to the existing `where_ref_value` path, tests cover empty `_2` COALESCE/NULLIF projection behavior, and final independent Codex re-review reported no high/medium findings.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent string functions)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` SQL parsing now supports the implemented AWS string-function subset for value expressions: `LOWER`, `UPPER`, `CHAR_LENGTH`, `CHARACTER_LENGTH`, `SUBSTRING`, and `TRIM`. ## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent CAST projections and aliases)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` SQL parsing now supports `CAST(ref AS INT/FLOAT)` as a normal projection expression and as a `WHERE` left-hand value expression, not only inside aggregate arguments. CSV output remains positional and does not emit header names, while JSON output uses explicit projection aliases when present and falls back to derived field names or positional `_N` names for expression/aggregate projections.

- JSON Lines Select execution now evaluates CAST projections and predicates, emits cast JSON numbers for successful CAST projections, and rejects duplicate JSON projection output names before event-stream 200. A runtime duplicate-name guard also remains in the projector as a second fail-closed defense.
- Mixed aggregate/non-aggregate validation now unwraps aliased projection nodes so aliases and CAST projections cannot bypass the existing `NotImplemented` guard. Referenced-field collection also unwraps aliases and CAST expressions, preserving CSV header validation, ScanRange/header fail-closed behavior, and JSON path/qualifier validation.
- Regression coverage now exercises parser ASTs for CAST projection/WHERE aliases and decimal literals, CSV CAST projection/WHERE, CSV header-name CAST, CSV aliases preserving positional output, CSV nonnumeric CAST fail-closed behavior, JSON CAST projection/WHERE aliases, JSON duplicate alias/natural/aggregate output-name rejection, and JSON nonnumeric CAST fail-closed behavior.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent WHERE predicates)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` SQL `WHERE` now supports boolean expressions with `AND`, `OR`, prefix `NOT`, and parentheses for the implemented CSV and JSON Lines executor paths. The parser preserves `AND` precedence over `OR`, treats parenthesized expressions as atoms, and keeps simple comparison ASTs compatible with prior projection/filtering code.
- JSON Lines `WHERE` now supports `IS NULL`, `IS NOT NULL`, `IN`, `NOT IN`, `BETWEEN`, `NOT BETWEEN`, and `LIKE ... ESCAPE` in addition to the prior `IS MISSING` / `IS NOT MISSING` predicates. JSON `null` no longer coerces to the string `"null"` for ordinary comparisons; null/missing ordinary predicate outcomes use three-valued logic so `NOT missing_field = 'x'` does not silently match missing rows.
- CSV `WHERE` now supports `IS NULL`, `IS NOT NULL`, `IN`, `NOT IN`, `BETWEEN`, `NOT BETWEEN`, and `LIKE ... ESCAPE` for the supported CSV executor path. Empty CSV fields are treated as SQL null for `WHERE` predicate evaluation while projections still serialize them as empty CSV fields; missing fields remain non-matching even under `NOT`.
- CSV continues to reject JSON-only `IS MISSING` / `IS NOT MISSING` predicates before event-stream 200, including nested boolean expressions, with structured `NotImplemented`. New predicate evaluation stays inside the existing staged-result contract, so source-length, ScanRange, GZIP, LIMIT, quoted CSV, and output-size safety paths remain shared.
- Regression coverage now exercises boolean precedence and parentheses, JSON null vs string `"null"`, CSV empty-field null handling, `NOT` with missing/null comparison operands, `IN`/`NOT IN`, numeric `BETWEEN`/`NOT BETWEEN`, `LIKE` wildcard and explicit `ESCAPE`, plus existing CSV fail-closed `IS MISSING` behavior.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent JSON FROM paths and missing predicates)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` JSON Lines execution now supports JSON `FROM S3Object[*]` paths beyond the root row: field paths, bracket field names, numeric indexes, array wildcards, and object wildcards can expand one physical JSON line into multiple logical Select rows.
- Wildcard expansion follows the AWS S3 Select JSON row model for this implemented subset. Missing path results are explicit `MISSING` rows that serialize as `{}` records, paths ending in a named field can be referenced by that last field name, and wildcard-ended paths use `_1` row references including direct row projection and bracket indexing.
- JSON path expansion is lazy across wildcard members, so `LIMIT` can stop without materializing every row in a selected JSON line. Existing ScanRange behavior still makes record-start decisions before parsing skipped records, preserving the oversized/malformed skipped-record safety contract.
- The SQL subset now supports `WHERE <ref> IS MISSING` and `WHERE <ref> IS NOT MISSING` for JSON Select rows, allowing callers to filter the empty `{}` records emitted by wildcard paths. CSV rejects the JSON-only missing predicates before event-stream 200 with structured `NotImplemented`, avoiding silent mis-evaluation or executor crashes.
- Existing JSON Lines projection/filtering/LIMIT, GZIP, custom record delimiter, source-length checks, ambiguous field handling, and JSON DOCUMENT fail-closed behavior remain intact.
- Regression coverage now exercises JSON FROM array/object wildcard expansion, bracket field paths, row-name references, `_1` direct and indexed row references, invalid prior-field row qualifiers, `IS MISSING`/`IS NOT MISSING`, CSV fail-closed missing predicates, and parser AST coverage for the new SQL forms.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent AllowQuotedRecordDelimiter support)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` CSV input now supports `AllowQuotedRecordDelimiter=true` for the implemented CSV executor path. The record-boundary scanner tracks quoted-field state and ignores `RecordDelimiter` bytes inside quoted fields, so multiline CSV fields are selected as one logical record.
- The scanner preserves quote/escape state across source chunks, including doubled default quotes split at chunk boundaries. Selected records remain bounded by existing `OverMaxRecordSize` / `ColumnTooLong` checks, while records skipped by `ScanRange`, `LIMIT 0`, or non-needed headers are boundary-scanned without parsing or size-checking their field contents.
- Default `AllowQuotedRecordDelimiter=false` behavior stays fail-closed: embedded record delimiters inside selected CSV records still return structured `NotImplemented`, and malformed quoted CSV continues to return structured XML before event-stream 200.
- The implementation keeps prior quoted CSV, CRLF normalization, GZIP, ScanRange, and source-length contracts intact. `ScanRange` still uses logical record start offsets, so a range starting after a multiline quoted record skips the whole prior record even if that prior record contains delimiter bytes.
- Regression coverage now exercises multiline quoted CSV selection, ScanRange skipping of an oversized multiline quoted record without parsing it, and quote escape pairs split across source chunks.
- Independent Codex review reported no high/medium findings for the batch.
