## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent quoted CSV input support)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` CSV input now supports quoted fields for the implemented CSV executor path. Quoted fields can contain field delimiters as data, doubled default quotes are unescaped, and custom `QuoteEscapeCharacter` is honored before projection/filtering/output formatting.
- `FileHeaderInfo=USE` now works with quoted CSV header rows and quoted SQL identifiers, so header-name projections and filters can bind names such as `"Display Name"` from a quoted header record.
- Default newline `RecordDelimiter` now accepts CRLF object data by stripping the trailing carriage return at physical record boundaries before CSV field parsing. The selected output remains governed by the configured output serialization, not by the source line ending.
- Malformed quoted CSV remains fail-closed before event-stream 200: unterminated or structurally invalid quoted fields return structured XML `InvalidArgument`. `AllowQuotedRecordDelimiter=true` remains explicitly `NotImplemented`, so embedded record delimiters inside quoted fields are not silently accepted until that stateful multi-line slice is implemented.
- Existing Select safety contracts remain in place: records skipped by `ScanRange`/`LIMIT 0` are not parsed or size-checked, non-ASCII CSV remains fail-closed, source-length checks still happen before event-stream responses, and GZIP/ScanRange behavior from prior batches is unchanged.
- Regression coverage now exercises quoted data fields with delimiters and escaped quotes, quoted header rows with quoted SQL identifiers, CRLF input records, custom quote escape handling, malformed quoted CSV pre-response XML errors, and `AllowQuotedRecordDelimiter=true` fail-closed behavior.
- Independent Codex review reported no high/medium findings for the batch.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent GZIP compression support)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` now supports `CompressionType=GZIP` for the implemented CSV and JSON Lines input paths. Object bytes are wrapped before the existing CSV/JSON executors, so projection/filtering/LIMIT/output serialization reuse the same request contract while storage still sees compressed bytes.
- GZIP decompression is implemented as a lazy `Stream.resource` over the source enumerable, using `:zlib.safeInflate/2` with gzip reset mode. It emits bounded decompressed chunks one at a time, preserves concatenated gzip members, halts upstream continuations on cleanup, and avoids per-object or per-compressed-chunk full inflated buffering.
- Compressed source integrity is explicit: the wrapper tracks compressed bytes read from storage, and the handler uses that counter for object source-length checks. For compressed `LIMIT`, CSV/JSON staging drains the compressed stream to EOF before sending event-stream 200 so gzip footers, truncation, concatenated members, and source length are validated before the response.
- Unsupported compressed surfaces remain fail-closed before event-stream: `CompressionType=BZIP2` returns structured `NotImplemented`, invalid GZIP and truncated GZIP return structured `InvalidRequest`, and `ScanRange` with compressed input is rejected as `InvalidRequest` because AWS only supports ScanRange on uncompressed CSV/JSON Lines/Parquet inputs.
- Regression coverage now exercises GZIP CSV, GZIP JSON Lines, concatenated gzip members, GZIP `LIMIT`, truncated GZIP with and without `LIMIT`, invalid GZIP, compressed source-length mismatch, GZIP + ScanRange rejection, and BZIP2 fail-closed behavior.
- Independent Codex review initially found high/medium issues around inflated buffering, truncated GZIP with LIMIT, and concatenated gzip members. Fixes moved decompression to a bounded lazy resource, made compressed LIMIT drain to EOF, and enabled gzip reset mode; final independent Codex review reported no high/medium findings.
- Verification: `mix format lib/maxio/s3/select_compression.ex lib/maxio/s3/handlers/select_object_content.ex lib/maxio/s3/select_csv.ex lib/maxio/s3/select_json.ex test/maxio/s3/select_object_content_e2e_test.exs`; `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`; focused `mix test test/maxio/s3/select_sql_test.exs test/maxio/s3/event_stream_test.exs test/maxio/s3/request_parsers/select_object_content_test.exs test/maxio/s3/select_object_content_e2e_test.exs test/maxio/s3/dispatcher_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 98 tests, 0 failures; adjacent `mix test test/maxio/s3/integration_test.exs test/maxio/s3/authz_test.exs test/maxio/s3/authz_matrix_test.exs test/maxio/s3/identity_policy_authz_test.exs test/maxio/s3/session_token_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 104 tests, 0 failures; final `mix precommit` -> 2899 tests, 0 failures (5 excluded).

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent ScanRange support)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` now supports `ScanRange` for the implemented uncompressed CSV and JSON Lines paths. `Start`/`End` use AWS's 0-based inclusive record-start semantics; Start-only scans from that byte to EOF; End-only is normalized as the last N object bytes from metadata size.
- The Select request parser now captures `ScanRange` bounds and rejects malformed range XML such as empty ranges or negative bounds. `Start > End` is rejected as structured `InvalidArgument` before any event-stream 200 response.
- CSV and JSON Lines executors now make ScanRange/LIMIT decisions before buffering a normal record. Records before `Start`, after finite `End`, or outside `LIMIT 0` are not parsed, decoded, size-checked, or staged, so malformed/quoted/oversized skipped records cannot leak into pre-response errors. CSV `FileHeaderInfo=IGNORE` and `USE` positional queries skip header rows without parsing when header names are not required; `FileHeaderInfo=USE` with header-name references plus ScanRange remains fail-closed as `NotImplemented`.
- Source-length integrity remains explicit: normal EOF paths still compare processed source bytes against object metadata, while finite ScanRange and LIMIT paths bypass that check only when the executor proves it halted before EOF with `halt_reason: :scan_range` or `:limit`.
- Unsupported Select surfaces still fail closed before event-stream: compression other than NONE, Parquet, JSON DOCUMENT, non-empty JSON `FROM` paths, quoted CSV input, and header-name CSV ScanRange queries.
- Independent Codex review found high/medium issues around buffering skipped ScanRange records, `LIMIT 0`, and CSV `FileHeaderInfo` edge cases. The fixes moved decisions to the executor byte-scan boundary and added oversized/malformed skipped-record regressions; final independent Codex review reported no high/medium findings.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent JSON Lines support)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` now supports JSON Lines input/output for the bounded SQL subset: `Type=LINES`, JSON output `RecordDelimiter`, `SELECT *`, explicit projections, aliases, `WHERE`, `LIMIT`, `FROM S3Object[*]`, no-alias `_1` row references, nested object fields, quoted JSON keys, array indexes, and JSON `null` values. JSON `DOCUMENT`, Parquet, compression, ScanRange, and non-empty JSON `FROM` key paths remain fail-closed before any event-stream 200 response.
- The SQL parser now carries structured path segments for JSON refs while preserving CSV behavior. CSV explicitly rejects the JSON-only `S3Object[*]` wildcard with `InvalidKeyPath`, so CSV and JSON capabilities stay separated at the executor boundary.
- JSON field resolution follows S3 Select-style case-insensitive unquoted names while preserving quoted-key exactness. Ambiguous unquoted JSON fields return `AmbiguousFieldName`; malformed JSON Lines return `JSONParsingError`; invalid JSON type/options return structured S3 XML errors.
- Source-length integrity is tightened for Select staging: normal reads compare processed source bytes against object metadata before `send_chunked(200)`, while `LIMIT` skips that check only when the executor proved it halted before EOF. Regressions cover non-LIMIT truncation plus both unterminated and delimited truncated final-record `LIMIT` cases.
- Select result staging remains pre-response to preserve XML errors before event-stream 200, but it is now bounded by `:s3, :select_result_max_bytes` (default 128 MiB) and writes selected output to private temp files, not memory. Overflow returns structured `InvalidRequest` before event-stream. Delete-marker Select responses now preserve `x-amz-delete-marker` while returning structured XML (`NoSuchKey`) instead of an empty body.
- Independent Codex review found high/medium issues around `LIMIT` source-length masking, CSV wildcard syntax, `_1` wildcard JSON refs, JSON array `null`, JSON delimiter preservation, output staging bounds, and delete-marker XML. Fixes were applied at the parser/executor/handler contract level; final independent Codex re-review reported no high/medium findings.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent CSV SQL/options extension)

- S3 `SelectObjectContent` CSV execution now supports a bounded SQL subset beyond `SELECT *`: positional projections such as `_2, _1`, quoted/header-name projections under `FileHeaderInfo=USE`, simple equality and numeric ordered `WHERE` predicates, table aliases, and `LIMIT`. The parser enforces the AWS Select 256 KiB SQL expression ceiling and keeps unsupported SQL/functions fail-closed as structured `NotImplemented`.
- CSV input/output options are now honored for the supported unquoted ASCII subset: `FileHeaderInfo=NONE|USE|IGNORE`, single-byte `FieldDelimiter`, `RecordDelimiter`, `QuoteCharacter`, `QuoteEscapeCharacter`, `Comments`, and output `QuoteFields=ALWAYS|ASNEEDED`. Unsupported JSON/Parquet, compression, ScanRange, quoted input fields, `AllowQuotedRecordDelimiter=true`, CRLF, non-ASCII bytes, and embedded record delimiters still fail before any event-stream 200 response.
- Header binding now validates missing and ambiguous header names immediately after the header row is consumed, including header-only inputs and `LIMIT 0` shapes. Missing/ambiguous names map to `MissingHeaderName` / `AmbiguousFieldName` XML errors, not delayed mid-stream failures.
- Select output amplification is bounded: explicit projection count and emitted column count are capped, SQL expression size is capped, and input/result column or record size overflow maps to structured Select errors such as `ColumnTooLong`, `OverMaxColumn`, and `OverMaxRecordSize`. Valid `LIMIT` short-circuiting now carries an explicit `halt_reason: :limit`, so the handler no longer misclassifies early source termination as an object length corruption 500.
- Coverage now exercises projection, filtering, large-object `LIMIT` short-circuiting, `FileHeaderInfo USE/IGNORE`, default comment skipping, custom delimiters/output quoting, missing and ambiguous headers, projection-count and expanded-record bounds, expression-size bounds, oversized input records, and prior Select/SSE/temp cleanup behavior.
- Independent Codex review found one High output-amplification issue and two Medium issues around `LIMIT` source-length checking and header validation timing. The implementation was fixed at the executor/handler contract level, and independent Codex re-review reported no blocking/high/medium findings remain.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 S3 SelectObjectContent foundation)

- S3 object `POST ?select&select-type=2` now dispatches to a dedicated `SelectObjectContent` handler and maps authorization to `s3:GetObject`. The implementation follows the documented request surface from AWS SelectObjectContent / REST SelectObject appendix and Smithy Amazon Event Stream docs: authenticated request only, `select-type=2` required, Range and response header overrides rejected, and `versionId` rejected explicitly for this first slice.
- First supported execution path is intentionally narrow and fail-closed: `ExpressionType=SQL`, normalized `SELECT * FROM S3Object`, uncompressed CSV input, CSV output, no `ScanRange`, and no custom CSV serialization options. Unsupported expressions, JSON/Parquet, compression, ScanRange, quoted fields, CRLF, non-ASCII bytes, embedded delimiters, or records over 1 MiB return structured S3 `NotImplemented` before any event-stream 200 response is sent.
- The response path now emits AWS event-stream frames with prelude CRC and message CRC plus `Records`, optional `Progress`, `Stats`, and `End` events. `Progress` and `Stats` payloads include the AWS-shaped `Details` wrapper with `BytesScanned`, `BytesProcessed`, and `BytesReturned`.
- Select staging uses a new `Maxio.S3.TempFile` helper that creates a per-result random private temp directory, chmods the directory to `0700` before payload creation, opens the payload file with `:exclusive`, chmods the file to `0600` fail-closed before writing, and returns an explicit cleanup closure. The handler invokes cleanup after event streaming and on pre-response source-length mismatch.
- SSE-S3/KMS/DSSE/SSE-C Select reads now decrypt as streams directly into the CSV selector; there is no separate plaintext source temp file. The selected output is staged once before `send_chunked(200)`, so CSV/decoder errors remain structured XML errors rather than mid-stream failures. Successful Select responses intentionally do not emit `x-amz-version-id`, and current delete markers return the Select delete-marker response without exposing a version id header.
- Coverage now exercises simple Select success, RequestProgress ordering, event-stream CRC/header encoding, Stats/Progress Details XML, SSE-S3 plaintext selection with temp cleanup, anonymous rejection despite public `GetObject`, missing `select-type`, explicit `versionId` rejection, versioned-current and delete-marker version-header non-exposure, unsupported expression/CSV cases, invalid RequestProgress parsing, huge single backend chunk with newline rejected before oversized concatenation, non-ASCII bytes as structured errors, and explicit staged cleanup of the private temp directory.
- Independent Codex review found and drove fixes for SSE plaintext double-staging, huge single-record buffering, version-id exposure, Stats/Progress XML shape, invalid UTF-8 crash risk, invalid RequestProgress parsing, and temp-file security/cleanup. Final independent Codex re-review reported no blocking/high/medium findings in the remediated Select files. Residual intentional limits: only simple ASCII-compatible unquoted CSV and `SELECT * FROM S3Object` are implemented; full SQL projection/filtering, JSON/Parquet, compression, ScanRange, custom CSV options, and live aws-cli interoperability remain future slices.
- Verification: `mix format lib/maxio/s3/temp_file.ex lib/maxio/s3/event_stream.ex lib/maxio/s3/request_parsers/select_object_content.ex lib/maxio/s3/select_csv.ex lib/maxio/s3/handlers/select_object_content.ex lib/maxio/s3/dispatcher.ex lib/maxio/s3/handlers.ex lib/maxio/policy/action_map.ex test/maxio/s3/event_stream_test.exs test/maxio/s3/request_parsers/select_object_content_test.exs test/maxio/s3/select_object_content_e2e_test.exs test/maxio/s3/dispatcher_test.exs`; `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`; focused `mix test test/maxio/s3/event_stream_test.exs test/maxio/s3/request_parsers/select_object_content_test.exs test/maxio/s3/select_object_content_e2e_test.exs test/maxio/s3/dispatcher_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 40 tests, 0 failures; adjacent `mix test test/maxio/s3/integration_test.exs test/maxio/s3/authz_test.exs test/maxio/s3/authz_matrix_test.exs test/maxio/s3/identity_policy_authz_test.exs test/maxio/s3/session_token_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 104 tests, 0 failures; final `mix precommit` -> 2841 tests, 0 failures (5 excluded).

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 IAM SSHPublicKey Query API)

- IAM Query API now implements root-only `UploadSSHPublicKey`, `ListSSHPublicKeys`, `GetSSHPublicKey`, `UpdateSSHPublicKey`, and `DeleteSSHPublicKey`. The actions preserve authenticated-root authorization, SigV4 `iam` service-scope signing, unsupported-parameter rejection, structured IAM Query XML, and shared list pagination.
- Core IAM now persists `iam_ssh_public_keys` with a Chinese-commented migration and a dedicated `Maxio.IAM.SSHPublicKey` schema. SSH public keys are scoped to explicit IAM users only; upload requires an active user, while list/get/update/delete require an existing user scope and return `NoSuchEntity` for wrong user/key scope.
- Upload accepts OpenSSH `ssh-rsa` and PEM RSA public keys, canonicalizes storage to OpenSSH SSH plus SubjectPublicKeyInfo PEM (`BEGIN PUBLIC KEY`), stores the OpenSSH blob MD5 fingerprint, and rejects malformed/non-canonical SSH mpints, invalid/even RSA exponents, non-RSA keys, and RSA moduli below 2048 bits.
- Duplicate SSH public keys are detected per user by canonical fingerprint before quota checks and now return AWS-aligned `DuplicateSSHPublicKey` HTTP 400. The per-user quota is five SSH public keys, enforced under a per-user Postgres advisory transaction lock.
- `GetSSHPublicKey` now requires `Encoding` and accepts only `SSH` or `PEM`; missing encoding returns structured `InvalidParameterValue`, and unsupported encodings return `UnrecognizedPublicKeyEncoding`. `UpdateSSHPublicKey` accepts only `Active` / `Inactive`.
- User deletion semantics now account for SSH public keys: IAM Query `DeleteUser` uses strict blockers and returns `DeleteConflict` while a user owns SSH public keys; the existing local `delete_user/1` cleanup helper is documented as cascading subordinate credentials/policies and now has a regression covering SSH key cleanup.
- Independent Codex review found and drove fixes for SSH mpint canonicalization, RSA exponent validation, AWS PEM output shape, required `Encoding`, and `DuplicateSSHPublicKey` status. Final Codex re-review reported no blocking/high correctness issues. Residual low-risk differences: locally generated `SSHPublicKeyId` validation remains narrower than AWS's public `[\w]+`/20..128 contract, and AWS docs are internally inconsistent on the `UploadSSHPublicKey` result member wrapper (`SSHPublicKey` table vs `PublicKey` sample).
- Verification: `mix format priv/repo/migrations/20260702174535_create_iam_ssh_public_keys.exs lib/maxio/iam/ssh_public_key.ex lib/maxio/iam.ex lib/maxio/iam/query_error.ex lib/maxio/iam/query_plug.ex lib/maxio/iam/query_view.ex test/maxio/iam/query_api_test.exs test/maxio/iam_test.exs`; `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`; focused SSH public key tests -> 3 Query tests, 0 failures; `mix test test/maxio/iam_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 43 tests, 0 failures; adjacent `mix test test/maxio/iam/query_api_test.exs test/maxio/iam_test.exs test/maxio/sts/query_api_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 165 tests, 0 failures; final `mix precommit` -> 2820 tests, 0 failures (5 excluded).

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-03 IAM SigningCertificate Query API)

- IAM Query API now implements root-only `UploadSigningCertificate`, `ListSigningCertificates`, `UpdateSigningCertificate`, and `DeleteSigningCertificate`. The actions preserve authenticated-root authorization, SigV4 `iam` service-scope signing, unsupported-parameter rejection, shared pagination, structured logging, and AWS-shaped IAM Query XML.
- Core IAM now persists `iam_signing_certificates` with a Chinese-commented migration and a dedicated `Maxio.IAM.SigningCertificate` schema. Signing certificates are principal-scoped credentials for root or explicit IAM users, not server certificate metadata: no path, ARN, tags, private key, or certificate chain is accepted or persisted.
- Upload validates a single PEM X.509 certificate, enforces the AWS 16 KiB certificate body limit and IAM character range, rejects expired/malformed certs fail-closed, stores a SHA256 DER fingerprint, and returns `DuplicateCertificate` for globally duplicated certificate bodies before checking the per-principal quota.
- Root and IAM-user certificate lifecycles are scoped independently. `UserName` omitted targets root; explicit `UserName` targets an active IAM user for upload and an existing IAM user for list/update/delete. Wrong user/certificate scope maps to `NoSuchEntity`; each principal is limited to two signing certificates under a principal-scoped Postgres advisory transaction lock.
- User deletion semantics now account for signing certificates: strict `DeleteUser` returns `DeleteConflict` while a user owns signing certificates, non-strict internal deletion cleans them up, and the DB foreign key uses `ON UPDATE CASCADE` so user rename keeps certificate ownership aligned.
- `GetAccountSummary` now reports `AccountSigningCertificatesPresent` for root signing certificates and `SigningCertificatesPerUserQuota = 2`, replacing the prior zero placeholders.
- Independent Codex review found one Medium issue: duplicate uploads at quota returned `LimitExceeded` before `DuplicateCertificate`. The upload path now validates/extracts fingerprint, checks duplicate fingerprint, then checks quota; a regression covers duplicate-at-quota. Follow-up Codex re-review reported `APPROVED`.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-02 IAM ServerCertificate Query API)

- IAM Query API now implements root-only `UploadServerCertificate`, `GetServerCertificate`, `ListServerCertificates`, `UpdateServerCertificate`, `DeleteServerCertificate`, `TagServerCertificate`, `UntagServerCertificate`, and `ListServerCertificateTags`. The actions preserve SigV4 `iam` service-scope signing, authenticated-root authorization, duplicate/unsupported parameter rejection, bounded POST form parsing, structured logging, and AWS-shaped IAM Query XML.
- Core IAM now persists `iam_server_certificates` with a Chinese-commented migration and a schema module. Server certificate names are case-insensitive unique in the local single account; ARNs preserve the full IAM path body; `GetAccountSummary` now reports `ServerCertificates` and `ServerCertificatesQuota`.
- Upload validates PEM certificate material with `:public_key`, checks that the leaf X.509 certificate public key matches the supplied RSA private key, extracts certificate expiration for metadata XML, accepts optional PEM certificate chains, and never persists or returns the raw private key. Only a SHA256 private-key fingerprint is stored for local audit/debug context.
- `UploadServerCertificate` is POST-only so private keys cannot be accepted through GET query strings; POST forms still require header SigV4 IAM auth, form content type, exact payload hash, duplicate-form rejection, and the bounded IAM form body limit. `UpdateServerCertificate` now rejects empty updates both at the Query API and strict core facade.
- Server certificate tags use AWS-style case-sensitive tag keys, matching the instance-profile behavior. `Env` and `env` can coexist; `UntagServerCertificate` removes exact keys only; tag listing is paginated through the shared IAM tag page helper.
- Independent Codex review first found two High issues: GET `UploadServerCertificate` could put `PrivateKey` in the URL, and empty `UpdateServerCertificate` returned no-op success. Both were fixed with targeted regressions, and follow-up Codex re-review reported `APPROVED - no blocking/high-risk findings`.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-02 IAM Instance Profile Query API)

- IAM Query API now implements root-only `CreateInstanceProfile`, `GetInstanceProfile`, `ListInstanceProfiles`, `DeleteInstanceProfile`, `AddRoleToInstanceProfile`, `RemoveRoleFromInstanceProfile`, `ListInstanceProfilesForRole`, `TagInstanceProfile`, `UntagInstanceProfile`, and `ListInstanceProfileTags`. The actions preserve SigV4 `iam` service-scope signing, authenticated-root authorization, duplicate/unsupported parameter rejection, bounded POST form parsing, structured logging, and AWS-shaped IAM Query XML.
- Core IAM now persists `iam_instance_profiles` with a Chinese-commented migration and a schema module. Instance profile names remain case-insensitive unique in the local single account; ARNs preserve the full IAM path body; `GetAccountSummary` now reports `InstanceProfiles` and `InstanceProfilesQuota`; role deletion is protected while a role is referenced by any instance profile.
- Instance profile paths now follow IAM path semantics for create (`/` or printable-ASCII path ending in `/`) and list prefix semantics for `PathPrefix` without requiring a trailing `/`. Regression coverage includes non-root paths, `PathPrefix=/application_abc`, path characters such as `!`, and repeated slash preservation in both XML `Path` and generated ARN.
- Instance profile tags use AWS-style case-sensitive tag keys, unlike the local user/role case-insensitive merge/drop behavior. `CostCenter` and `costcenter`, or `ENV` and `env`, can coexist; `UntagInstanceProfile` removes exact keys only.
- Role membership is serialized through transaction-scoped `FOR UPDATE` row locks for add, remove, and delete. Re-adding the same role returns `EntityAlreadyExists`; adding a different second role returns `LimitExceeded`; deleting a profile that has a role returns `DeleteConflict`; concurrent AddRole/DeleteInstanceProfile cannot both succeed.
- Independent Codex review first found high issues around path/PathPrefix semantics and Delete/AddRole race, plus a medium issue where ARN generation trimmed repeated slashes from legal paths. All findings were fixed with targeted regressions, and final Codex re-review reported `APPROVED`.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-02 IAM PermissionsBoundary Query API / Authz)

- IAM permissions boundaries are now implemented for local IAM users and roles. `CreateUser` / `CreateRole` accept `PermissionsBoundary`, and the IAM Query API now supports `PutUserPermissionsBoundary`, `DeleteUserPermissionsBoundary`, `PutRolePermissionsBoundary`, and `DeleteRolePermissionsBoundary` for root operators while preserving SigV4 `iam` service-scope, authenticated-root authorization, duplicate parameter rejection, bounded form parsing, and structured IAM Query XML.
- Core IAM persists `permissions_boundary_policy_arn` on `iam_users` and `iam_roles` with a Chinese-commented migration and schema validations. Public user/role projections and Query XML now include AWS-shaped `PermissionsBoundaryType=PermissionsBoundaryPolicy` and `PermissionsBoundaryArn` when set. Boundary policy ARNs must be valid local managed-policy ARNs and must reference an existing managed policy; missing policies map to `NoSuchEntity`.
- Managed-policy metadata and entity lookup now count and expose permissions-boundary usage. `GetPolicy` / `ListPolicies` report real `PermissionsBoundaryUsageCount`; `PolicyUsageFilter=PermissionsBoundary` returns policies currently used as boundaries; `ListEntitiesForPolicy` returns boundary users/roles for that usage filter; and `DeletePolicy` returns `DeleteConflict` while a policy is used as a boundary.
- S3 authorization now evaluates permissions boundaries as maximum permissions for IAM users/roles and assumed-role principals. Identity-policy allows must intersect with the boundary, boundary explicit denies override resource-policy/ACL/owner allows, and same-account resource grants directly to IAM user ARNs still bypass only boundary implicit deny. The former non-root owner shortcut is now routed through boundary evaluation; root remains the root owner bypass.
- STS caller authorization for `AssumeRole`, `TagSession`, and `SetSourceIdentity` now honors caller permissions boundaries. Account-principal trust requires caller identity allow intersected with boundary allow; direct principal trust still does not require caller identity allow but now fails closed on caller identity explicit deny, boundary explicit deny, or invalid boundary.
- Independent Codex review found two High issues in the first implementation: non-root S3 owner shortcut bypassed boundary explicit deny, and STS caller authorization ignored permissions boundaries. Both were fixed with regressions for owner explicit deny, account-principal AssumeRole boundary implicit deny, direct-principal trust boundary explicit deny, and TagSession/SetSourceIdentity boundary gating. Residual risk: no live AWS probe for exact XML element ordering or edge-case cross-account boundary semantics; local single-account behavior is covered.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-02 IAM Role Update Query API)

- IAM Query API now implements root-only `UpdateAssumeRolePolicy` and `UpdateRole`, alongside `CreateRole` / `GetRole` role `Description` parity. The actions are dispatched in `Maxio.IAM.QueryPlug`, preserve the existing SigV4 `iam` service-scope and root-operator gates, reject unsupported operation parameters, and return AWS-shaped IAM Query XML.
- Core IAM now exposes strict role update facades for trust policy and role attributes. `Description` is persisted on `iam_roles`, included in public role projections and role XML when non-empty, and backed by text-column migrations with Chinese schema comments plus a follow-up widen migration for already-migrated local databases.
- Trust policy document handling now fail-closes before persistence on malformed JSON, duplicate JSON object keys at any depth, invalid IAM policy document characters, and over-size documents. Role descriptions enforce the AWS-compatible 0..1000 character range and allowed character set in both Query parsing and schema validation.
- Coverage exercises role description create/get/update XML, max-session-duration update, `UpdateAssumeRolePolicy` success, malformed trust policy rejection, recursive duplicate-key rejection, unsupported/wrong-action rejection, missing-role mapping, invalid/overlong description rejection, and unchanged role state after rejected trust policy updates. The adjacent STS `GetAccessKeyInfo` missing-key fixture was also made stable by generating syntactically valid unknown access keys.
- Independent Codex review first found missing `UpdateRole Description`, trust-policy duplicate-key acceptance, and missing trust-policy size/charset validation; a follow-up Codex review then found the migration needed `:text` rather than `varchar(255)`. All findings were fixed, and final Codex re-review reported `APPROVED`.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-02 IAM UpdateUser / UpdateGroup Query API)

- IAM Query API now implements root-only `UpdateUser` and `UpdateGroup`. The actions are dispatched in `Maxio.IAM.QueryPlug`, preserve authenticated-root authorization, enforce SigV4 `iam` service-scope signing, reject unsupported operation parameters, and return AWS-shaped IAM Query XML (`UpdateUserResult/User` for `UpdateUser`, empty success for `UpdateGroup`).
- The implementation was checked against the AWS IAM API reference for `UpdateUser` and `UpdateGroup`. The current local IAM model only supports the root path `/`, so `NewPath=/` is accepted and all other valid IAM paths fail closed with `InvalidParameterValue` rather than being silently accepted without persistence.
- User/group rename is implemented in core IAM with transaction-scoped ARN migration. `iam_users.user_arn` and `iam_groups.group_arn` remain the local business keys; rename now updates the primary row and preserves all local references for access keys, inline policies, memberships, managed-policy attachments, MFA devices, and STS session principals where applicable.
- A new migration upgrades IAM membership and managed-policy attachment foreign keys to `ON UPDATE CASCADE`, with Chinese constraint comments, so DB-enforced user/group ARN references follow parent rename atomically. Non-FK principal references are updated explicitly in the same core transaction and both old/new principals plus affected access/session keys are broadcast for cache invalidation.
- Coverage exercises duplicate/case-insensitive rename rejection, `NewPath` fail-closed semantics, `UpdateUser` XML shape, `UpdateGroup` empty success shape, old-name lookup failure, new-name lookup success, access-key principal migration, inline-policy ARN migration, group-membership migration, managed-policy attachment migration, and non-root denial. ## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-02 IAM Account Password Policy Query API)

- IAM Query API now implements root-only `UpdateAccountPasswordPolicy`, `GetAccountPasswordPolicy`, and `DeleteAccountPasswordPolicy`. The actions are dispatched in `Maxio.IAM.QueryPlug`, preserve authenticated-root authorization, enforce SigV4 `iam` service-scope signing, reject unsupported operation parameters, and return AWS-shaped IAM Query XML.
- The implementation was checked against the AWS IAM API reference for account password policies. `UpdateAccountPasswordPolicy` intentionally uses AWS's non-partial semantics: omitted parameters reset to documented defaults. `MaxPasswordAge` and `PasswordReusePrevention` persist as `NULL` for the AWS default zero/no-limit behavior and are omitted from `GetAccountPasswordPolicy` XML unless explicitly configured; `ExpirePasswords` is derived from `MaxPasswordAge > 0`.
- Account password policy state is persisted in a new singleton `iam_account_password_policies` table with Chinese table/column/index comments and DB-enforced constraints for singleton ownership, `MinimumPasswordLength` 6..128, `MaxPasswordAge` 1..1095 when present, and `PasswordReusePrevention` 1..24 when present.

Missing get/delete maps to `NoSuchEntity`, malformed booleans and out-of-range integers fail closed as IAM Query `InvalidParameterValue`, and the upsert path replaces the complete singleton row so omitted fields revert to defaults.
- Coverage exercises the default-policy create path, full update path, derived `ExpirePasswords`, omitted optional XML elements, invalid range/boolean rejection, unsupported-parameter rejection, non-root denial, missing get/delete failures, delete success, changeset range validation, and direct raw-SQL singleton bypass failure at the DB boundary. ## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-02 IAM Account Alias Query API)

- IAM Query API now implements root-only `CreateAccountAlias`, `ListAccountAliases`, and `DeleteAccountAlias`. The actions are dispatched in `Maxio.IAM.QueryPlug`, preserve authenticated-root authorization, enforce SigV4 `iam` service-scope signing, reject unsupported operation parameters, and return AWS-shaped IAM Query XML.
- The implementation was checked against the AWS IAM API reference for account aliases. `AccountAlias` follows the documented 3..63 character pattern for lowercase letters, digits, and single dashes, with no leading/trailing dash and no consecutive dashes. `ListAccountAliases` supports `MaxItems` / `Marker` pagination and renders `AccountAliases.member`, `IsTruncated`, and optional `Marker`.
- Account aliases are persisted in a new `iam_account_aliases` table with Chinese table/column/index comments. The AWS one-alias-per-account rule is enforced at the database boundary with both a singleton unique index and a `CHECK (singleton_key = 1)` constraint in a follow-up migration, so direct inserts cannot bypass the single-account invariant.
- Core IAM now exposes `create_account_alias/1`, `list_account_aliases/0`, and `delete_account_alias/1`. Duplicate same-alias creation maps to `EntityAlreadyExists`, trying to create a different second alias maps to `LimitExceeded`, malformed aliases fail closed, and missing deletes map to `NoSuchEntity`.
- Coverage exercises core lifecycle, malformed alias validation, duplicate/limit errors, direct schema-boundary singleton bypass via raw SQL, empty/non-empty Query XML lists, marker-after-last pagination, unsupported-parameter rejection, non-root denial, delete success, and missing-delete failure. Adjacent STS/S3 authorization tests were rerun to catch shared Query/auth regressions.
- Independent Codex review first found the singleton unique index was bypassable without a DB check constraint. A follow-up migration added `iam_account_aliases_singleton_key_is_one`, the core test now verifies raw SQL `singleton_key=2` fails, and independent Codex re-review reported `APPROVED`.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-02 IAM GetAccessKeyLastUsed Query API)

- IAM Query API now implements `GetAccessKeyLastUsed` for root operators. The action is dispatched in `Maxio.IAM.QueryPlug`, preserves the existing authenticated-root gate, enforces SigV4 `iam` service-scope signing, rejects unsupported parameters, and returns AWS-shaped IAM Query XML with `UserName` plus `AccessKeyLastUsed`.
- Access-key last-used metadata is now persisted on long-term access-key rows through `last_used_at`, `last_used_service`, and `last_used_region`. The new migration follows the schema self-documenting rule with Chinese column comments, and `Maxio.IAM.AccessKey` exposes the fields without adding secret material to public projections.
- Successful SigV4 authentication now records last-used metadata for long-term root/IAM-user credentials using the signed request service, signed request region, and request timestamp. STS session principals are explicitly skipped so temporary credentials do not update the issuer key, and the recorder only touches Repo when the configured credential backend is `Maxio.Auth.CredentialStore.EctoStore`; MemoryStore/custom backends keep the low-level authentication path DB-free.
- Unused keys return `ServiceName=N/A`, `Region=N/A`, and omit `LastUsedDate`; used keys return the recorded service/region/date. Missing or malformed `AccessKeyId` values map to structured IAM Query errors, while normal IDs follow AWS's `16..128` ASCII alnum/underscore shape and the configured local root key such as `minioadmin` remains an explicit compatibility exception.
- Coverage now exercises the strict IAM facade, unused/used XML shapes, last-used updates after a signed IAM request that authenticates but fails root authorization, missing/invalid/unknown `AccessKeyId` failures, configured local root-key compatibility, the SigV4 MemoryStore low-level plug path, and adjacent STS/S3 authorization paths.
- Independent Codex review of the initial implementation reported `APPROVED`. The first full `mix precommit` exposed a DB sandbox ownership failure in the MemoryStore-only SigV4 plug test; the implementation was tightened to persist last-used only under the Ecto credential backend, focused tests passed, and follow-up independent Codex re-review reported `APPROVED`.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-02 STS GetAccessKeyInfo Query API)

- STS Query API now implements `GetAccessKeyInfo` instead of treating it as an unsupported STS action or allowing it to fall through to S3 `ListBuckets`. The action is dispatched in `Maxio.STS.QueryPlug`, requires authenticated SigV4 credentials, enforces STS service-scope signing, and returns a structured STS XML response with the configured single-account id.
- Core STS now exposes `Maxio.STS.access_key_account_id/1` and `account_id/0`. The account lookup covers both long-term IAM access keys and STS session access keys, including inactive/revoked rows, because AWS STS `GetAccessKeyInfo` identifies the owning account only and must not disclose key state, principal ARN, secret envelopes, or session-token validity.
- `AccessKeyId` validation now follows the AWS STS shape for normal keys (`16..128` ASCII alnum/underscore) while preserving explicit local MinIO-style root compatibility for the currently configured `config :maxio, :s3, :root_user` such as `minioadmin`. Missing IDs return `InvalidParameterValue`; syntactically invalid IDs return `InvalidParameterValue`; syntactically valid unknown IDs return `InvalidClientTokenId`.
- Presigned GET requests are supported by allowing the SigV4 `X-Amz-*` query parameters during `GetAccessKeyInfo` unsupported-parameter filtering. Header-signed GET and POST form paths share the same validation and XML output, and POST responses do not expose `SecretAccessKey` or `SessionToken`.
- Coverage now exercises header-signed root lookup, presigned STS lookup, configured local root exception, POST form lookup for temporary credentials, missing/invalid/unknown `AccessKeyId` failures, no S3 fallback, and core long-term/session lookup behavior after inactive/revoked state changes.
- Independent Codex review first found the `AccessKeyId` validation was too broad versus AWS STS and lacked invalid/local-root regression coverage. The implementation was tightened to AWS-style validation with a configured-root exception, tests were added, and follow-up independent Codex re-review reported `APPROVED`.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-02 IAM AccessKey Lifecycle Query API)

- IAM long-term access-key lifecycle is now implemented on the IAM Query API path for root operators: `CreateAccessKey`, `ListAccessKeys`, `UpdateAccessKey`, and `DeleteAccessKey`. The new actions preserve the root-only gate, SigV4 `iam` service-scope requirement, duplicate-parameter rejection, bounded POST form parsing, strict unsupported-parameter rejection, and IAM Query XML namespace output.
- Core IAM now exposes strict access-key facade functions for Query semantics: `create_access_key_strict/1`, `list_access_keys_strict/1`, `update_access_key_strict/3`, and `delete_access_key_strict/2`. `nil` `UserName` targets the local root principal; explicit `UserName` targets an IAM user ARN and enforces exact principal scoping so a key cannot be updated or deleted through another user name.
- Access-key secret material remains one-time visible: `CreateAccessKey` returns `SecretAccessKey`, while `ListAccessKeys` and strict core listings never expose stored secret envelopes. Inactive or expired keys fail closed through `EctoStore.get_secret/1`, and `Status=Expired` is intentionally supported because the current AWS IAM `UpdateAccessKey` API reference lists `Active | Inactive | Expired` as valid values.
- Query XML now returns AWS-shaped `AccessKey` / `AccessKeyMetadata` members with `UserName`, `AccessKeyId`, `Status`, and `CreateDate`; `ListAccessKeys` supports `MaxItems` / `Marker` pagination, omits secrets, preserves requested `UserName` even for an existing user with zero keys, and maps missing users or wrong key/user scope to `NoSuchEntity`.
- The strict create path enforces the AWS two-access-key quota per principal under a principal-scoped Postgres advisory transaction lock. Strict update/delete use the same lock, and root lockout checks run in the same transaction as the mutation, so concurrent root `UpdateAccessKey` / `DeleteAccessKey` requests cannot leave zero active root credentials.
- Independent Codex review first found active-root delete protection, access-key quota atomicity, and root update/delete serialization gaps; all were fixed. A reviewer note to reject `Expired` was not applied after checking the AWS API reference; regression coverage now exercises `Status=Expired` and authentication fail-closed behavior. Final Codex re-review of the last P1 reported `APPROVED`.
- Verification: `mix format lib/maxio/iam.ex lib/maxio/iam/access_key.ex lib/maxio/iam/query_plug.ex lib/maxio/iam/query_view.ex test/maxio/iam_test.exs test/maxio/iam/query_api_test.exs`; scoped `git diff --check` passed with only the known STATUS CRLF normalization warning; `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`; focused `mix test test/maxio/iam_test.exs test/maxio/iam/query_api_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 67 tests, 0 failures; adjacent `mix test test/maxio/sts/query_api_test.exs test/maxio/s3/authz_matrix_test.exs test/maxio_web/live/access_key_live_test.exs test/maxio_web/live/sts_session_live_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 123 tests, 0 failures; final `mix precommit` -> 2787 tests, 0 failures (5 excluded).

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-02 OIDC Issuer / Discovery Redirect Hardening)

- OIDC issuer normalization now rejects URL userinfo at the shared `Maxio.STS.WebIdentity.issuer_host_path/1` boundary. This prevents issuer strings such as `https://evil@example.com/tenant` from aliasing the same provider ARN and DB lookup as `https://example.com/tenant`.
- IAM OIDC provider management inherits the same fail-closed issuer boundary through `oidc_provider_arn/1`, provider changesets, provider lookup, and WebIdentity verification. Direct `IAM.create_oidc_provider/1` with userinfo now fails before persisting a provider row.
- OIDC discovery and JWKS HTTP fetches now pass `redirect: false` to Req and treat 30x responses as `:oidc_discovery_http_error`. The thumbprint fallback HTTPS parser already did not follow redirects; this batch added an explicit regression so both public-CA Req and private-CA thumbprint paths stay fail-closed on redirects.
- The lower-level thumbprint fallback URL parser also rejects userinfo, keeping defense-in-depth if a future caller reaches that path with an unnormalized URL.
- Independent Codex review of only the OIDC hardening files reported `APPROVED`; reviewer reran focused checks for the userinfo and redirect regressions.
- Verification: `mix format lib/maxio/sts/web_identity.ex lib/maxio/iam/oidc_discovery/http.ex test/maxio/sts_test.exs test/maxio/iam_oidc_provider_test.exs test/maxio/iam/oidc_discovery/http_test.exs`; scoped `git diff --check` passed with only the known STATUS CRLF normalization warning; `mix compile --warnings-as-errors`; focused `mix test test/maxio/iam/oidc_discovery/http_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 5 tests, 0 failures; focused `mix test test/maxio/iam_oidc_provider_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 6 tests, 0 failures; focused `mix test test/maxio/sts_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 28 tests, 0 failures; adjacent `mix test test/maxio/sts/query_api_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 71 tests, 0 failures; adjacent `mix test test/maxio/iam/query_api_test.exs --max-failures 1` -> 29 tests, 0 failures; final `mix precommit` -> 2785 tests, 0 failures (5 excluded).

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-02 IAM Role Lifecycle Query API)

- IAM role lifecycle management is now implemented on the IAM Query API path for root operators: `CreateRole`, `GetRole`, `ListRoles`, `DeleteRole`, `TagRole`, `UntagRole`, and `ListRoleTags`. The new actions preserve the root-only gate, SigV4 `iam` service-scope requirement, duplicate-parameter rejection, bounded POST form parsing, strict unsupported-parameter rejection, and IAM Query XML namespace output.
- Core IAM now exposes strict role lifecycle facade functions: `get_role/1`, `delete_role_strict/1`, `tag_role/2`, `untag_role/2`, and `list_role_tags_strict/1`. Role names are case-insensitive for duplicate preflight and name lookup, with a new `lower(role_name)` unique index and Chinese index COMMENT to close the DB concurrency boundary.
- `CreateRole` validates the current root-only role path `/`, required and semantically valid trust policy JSON, `MaxSessionDuration` range, role name shape, and IAM tag shape. Role XML returns AWS-shaped metadata including `Path`, `RoleName`, synthetic stable `RoleId`, `Arn`, `CreateDate`, URL-encoded `AssumeRolePolicyDocument`, `MaxSessionDuration`, and detail tags where AWS returns them. Later batches added `Description` and `PermissionsBoundary` support.
- `DeleteRole` is strict for Query: missing roles map to `NoSuchEntity`, malformed role names map to `InvalidParameterValue`, and deletion returns `DeleteConflict` while inline policies, managed-policy attachments, or active unexpired STS sessions remain. The older backend `delete_role/1` remains the cascading/operator compatibility path.
- Independent Codex review first found two in-scope schema-boundary issues: direct role creation could persist a non-role/mismatched ARN, and direct role creation/tagging could bypass IAM tag rules. `Maxio.IAM.Role` now validates role ARN shape, ARN/name agreement, and IAM tag rules in the changeset, with direct core regressions for create/tag bypass paths. Follow-up Codex review: `APPROVED` and verified both findings closed. An earlier over-scoped Codex pass also flagged unrelated OIDC issuer userinfo and redirect-hardening risks; those are queued as the next separate security slice, not part of this role batch.

## Previous Batch Update (2026-07-02 IAM Role Inline Policy Query API)

- IAM role inline policy management is now implemented on the IAM Query API path for root operators: `PutRolePolicy`, `GetRolePolicy`, `ListRolePolicies`, and `DeleteRolePolicy`. The new actions preserve the root-only gate, SigV4 `iam` service-scope requirement, duplicate-parameter rejection, bounded POST form parsing, strict unsupported-parameter rejection, Store-behaviour boundary, and IAM Query XML namespace output.
- Role inline policies reuse the existing `principal_policies` Store contract through new strict `Maxio.IAM` facade functions: `put_role_policy_strict/3`, `get_role_policy_strict/2`, `list_role_policies_strict/1`, and `delete_role_policy_strict/2`. Strict Query semantics resolve missing roles and missing policy rows to `NoSuchEntity`; invalid `RoleName` / `PolicyName`, malformed policy documents, duplicate JSON keys, explicit identity-policy `Principal`, and unsupported params fail closed with structured IAM Query errors.

- Role inline aggregate policy quota is enforced at the IAM core path with the AWS 10,240-character role quota. The quota check and replacement write run under the same per-principal advisory transaction lock used for user/group inline policies, excluding the replaced same-name policy from the aggregate count so valid replacements do not falsely exceed quota.
